  756: def run(settings: JobSettings, source: pnezd.PnezdFile | None = None) -> JobResult:
  757:     """Execute a job. Reads if no parsed file is supplied; never writes.
  758: 
  759:     When ``source`` is supplied the job converts those rows and nothing else, so
  760:     the record's SHA-256 is that source's own digest - which is None unless the
  761:     source came from ``pnezd.read``. It is never taken from
  762:     ``settings.input_path``, because that path was not what was converted
  763:     (WP-R3 fix 2).
  764:     """
  765:     if settings.direction is Direction.ZONE_TO_ZONE:
  766:         if settings.source_zone is None or settings.target_zone is None:
  767:             raise ValueError(
  768:                 "A zone-to-zone conversion needs both a source and a target "
  769:                 "zone. Neither is inferred from the coordinates."
  770:             )
  771:     elif settings.direction is Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE:
  772:         if settings.target_zone is None:
  773:             raise ValueError("A geodetic conversion needs a target zone.")
  774:     elif settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY:
  775:         if settings.target_zone is not None:
  776:             raise ValueError(
  777:                 f"A vertical-only job was given target_zone "
  778:                 f"{settings.target_zone.name}, but no output horizontal "
  779:                 f"system exists in this mode: the only conversion performed "
  780:                 f"is the vertical datum shift, and the exports reproduce the "
  781:                 f"input's coordinate columns unchanged. State "
  782:                 f"target_zone=None; to convert the coordinates as well, run "
  783:                 f"a zone-to-zone job with "
  784:                 f"VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL."
  785:             )
  786:         if settings.output_unit != settings.input_unit:
  787:             raise ValueError(
  788:                 f"A vertical-only job's exports reproduce the input's "
  789:                 f"coordinate columns exactly, so its output unit must equal "
  790:                 f"its input unit - re-expressing "
  791:                 f"{settings.input_unit.code} values in "
  792:                 f"{settings.output_unit.code} would alter every column the "
  793:                 f"export promises to mirror. Got "
  794:                 f"input_unit={settings.input_unit.code}, "
  795:                 f"output_unit={settings.output_unit.code}. To change units, "
  796:                 f"run a horizontal or horizontal-and-vertical job, whose "
  797:                 f"exports state the output unit rather than mirroring the "
  798:                 f"input."
  799:             )
  800:         # ``source_zone`` is the input system: a Zone for a PNEZD file, or
  801:         # None for geodetic positions - exactly the existing source_zone
  802:         # idiom, so no third field exists to disagree with it.
  803:     elif settings.source_zone is None:
  804:         raise ValueError("Converting to geodetic needs the zone the file is in.")
  805: 
  806:     # Which jobs consult the longitude sign convention. A vertical-only job
  807:     # reads longitudes from the file ONLY when its input is geodetic; with a
  808:     # zone input the file carries none and none are written, so that job
  809:     # states None exactly as a zone-to-zone job does.
  810:     consults_longitude = settings.direction in (
  811:         Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE,
  812:         Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC,
  813:     ) or (
  814:         settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
  815:         and settings.source_zone is None
  816:     )
  817:     if consults_longitude and settings.longitude_convention is None:
  818:         raise ValueError(
  819:             "A conversion with geodetic coordinates on either end needs the "
  820:             "longitude sign convention the file uses. It has no default: the "
  821:             "manual writes Michigan's longitudes positive west and every GPS, "
  822:             "GIS and NGS tool writes them negative west, the two are "
  823:             "indistinguishable from the numbers alone, and choosing wrongly "
  824:             "moves a Michigan point about 340 miles."
  825:         )
  826:     if (
  827:         settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
  828:         and settings.source_zone is not None
  829:         and settings.longitude_convention is not None
  830:     ):
  831:         raise ValueError(
  832:             f"A vertical-only job reading State Plane coordinates was given "
  833:             f"a longitude sign convention "
  834:             f"({settings.longitude_convention.value!r}). The input file "
  835:             f"carries no longitude column and none is written - the pivot "
  836:             f"latitude and longitude are computed from the zone, in the "
  837:             f"program's own signed convention - so a convention stated here "
  838:             f"would be an answer to a question this job never asks, exactly "
  839:             f"as a zone-to-zone job states None. Pass "
  840:             f"longitude_convention=None."
  841:         )
  842: 
  843:     # Every vertical-settings refusal fires here, before any file is read and
  844:     # long before any point converts (docs/PLAN-vertical-datums.md section
  845:     # 3.5). None for a horizontal job; the registry's record for a vertical
  846:     # one.
  847:     transformation = _require_vertical_settings(settings)
  848: 
  849:     if source is None and settings.input_path is None:
  850:         # ``input_path`` is None only because the caller stated this job came
  851:         # from no file, and no rows arrived either - so there is nothing to
  852:         # convert and nothing to read. Refused rather than defaulted: a
  853:         # placeholder path here would send pnezd.read at a file nobody named
  854:         # (docs/DESIGN.md section 1, amendment #26).
  855:         raise ValueError(
  856:             "This job has no input file and no parsed rows, so there is "
  857:             "nothing to convert. Either give JobSettings an input_path, or "
  858:             "hand run() a source - michspc.fileio.pnezd.parse_typed_point "
  859:             "builds one from a single typed coordinate."
  860:         )
  861: 
  862:     parsed = source or pnezd.read(settings.input_path)
  863: 
  864:     if settings.geoid_model is not None and not isinstance(
  865:         settings.geoid_model, geoid.GeoidModel
  866:     ):
  867:         # The #11-finding-1 class, at this field's likeliest call site: the
  868:         # argument replaced ``apply_geoid: bool``, so ``geoid_model=True`` is
  869:         # the exact habit a caller carries forward - and truthiness would
  870:         # accept it here, then fail attribute-by-attribute somewhere inside
  871:         # the loader. if/raise, never assert (-O strips asserts).
  872:         raise TypeError(
  873:             f"JobSettings.geoid_model must be a michspc.fileio.geoid."
  874:             f"GeoidModel record, or None to state that no geoid is applied; "
  875:             f"got {type(settings.geoid_model).__name__} "
  876:             f"({settings.geoid_model!r}). In particular True is not 'the "
  877:             f"default model' - that was apply_geoid's contract, retired by "
  878:             f"WP-V5. Pass geoid.GEOID18_MODEL, geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL, or a "
  879:             f"record from geoid.geoid_model_by_name()."
  880:         )
 1140:     )
 1141: 
 1142:     points: list[ConvertedPoint] = []
 1143:     for row in parsed.rows:
 1144:         points.append(
 1145:             _convert_row(
 1146:                 row, settings, grid, transformation, vertcon_grids, swap_grids
 1147:             )
 1148:         )
 1149: 
 1150:     return JobResult(
 1151:         settings=settings,
 1152:         points=tuple(points),
 1153:         # The digest of the bytes the parser consumed, and nothing else. None
 1154:         # when the rows arrived already parsed - see JobResult.input_sha256.
 1155:         input_sha256=parsed.sha256,
 1156:         input_row_count=len(parsed.rows),
 1157:         skipped_blank_lines=parsed.skipped_blank_lines,
 1158:         # The NAME of the model the factors were actually computed from, not
 1159:         # the record: JobResult's contract predates the registry and
 1160:         # report.py resolves the record back through geoid_model_by_name.
 1161:         # For every pre-existing call shape this is settings.geoid_model's
 1162:         # own name; since the per-side feature it is the factors side's, so
 1163:         # a job whose only model is input-side (geoid_model=None,
 1164:         # source_geoid_model=GEOID18) names the grid its factors really
 1165:         # came from rather than claiming no geoid was applied.
 1166:         geoid_model=(
 1167:             factors_model.name if factors_model is not None else None
 1168:         ),
 1169:     )

 1183: 
 1184: def _require_vertical_settings(
 1185:     settings: JobSettings,
 1186: ) -> VerticalTransformation | None:
 1187:     """The vertical transformation this job will apply per point, or None.
 1188: 
 1189:     Every settings-level vertical refusal, in one place, before any point
 1190:     converts (docs/PLAN-vertical-datums.md section 3.5):
 1191: 
 1192:     * a vertical job missing either datum - refused naming which, in the style
 1193:       of the longitude-convention refusal;
 1194:     * a HORIZONTAL job SUPPLIED with either datum - refused too: a datum
 1195:       handed to a job that will never apply or record it is a contradiction,
 1196:       and silently ignoring it would let a caller believe an elevation
 1197:       conversion happened (the mirror of longitude_convention's None-is-a-
 1198:       statement rule);
 1199:     * a non-``VerticalDatum`` in either field - the #11-finding-1 impostor
 1200:       class, refused by name exactly as ``geoid_model`` is beside it;
 1201:     * ``require_vertical_pair``'s own two refusals (a datum declared but not
 1202:       usable; a pair with no published transformation) propagate UNTOUCHED -
 1203:       they already name the offending datum and teach, and wrapping them
 1204:       would hide the classes ``spc.vertical`` tells callers to catch.
 1205:     """
 1206:     mode = settings.vertical_mode
 1207:     if not isinstance(mode, VerticalMode):
 1208:         # The #11-finding-1 class for this field: `vertical_mode=True` is the
 1209:         # habit a boolean toggle teaches, and `is` comparison would silently
 1210:         # treat any impostor as a mode nobody chose. if/raise, never assert.
 1211:         raise TypeError(
 1212:             f"JobSettings.vertical_mode must be a michspc.job.VerticalMode; "
 1213:             f"got {type(mode).__name__} ({mode!r}). In particular True is "
 1214:             f"not 'vertical on': an impostor compares unequal to every "
 1215:             f"member and would be treated as whichever branch its identity "
 1216:             f"check happened to miss. Pass VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL, "
 1217:             f"VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL, or "
 1218:             f"VerticalMode.VERTICAL."
 1219:         )
 1220: 
 1221:     # VERTICAL and VERTICAL_ONLY require each other, in both directions.
 1222:     # A vertical-only direction under any other mode either converts nothing
 1223:     # (HORIZONTAL) or promises a horizontal conversion this direction does
 1224:     # not perform; a VERTICAL mode on any other direction would silently
 1225:     # decide whether that job's coordinates move. Refused rather than
 1226:     # reconciled, in the style of the rest of this matrix.
 1227:     if (
 1228:         settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
 1229:         and mode is not VerticalMode.VERTICAL
 1230:     ):
 1231:         raise ValueError(
 1232:             f"This job's direction is "
 1233:             f"{Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY.value!r} but its vertical_mode is "
 1234:             f"{mode.value!r}. A vertical-only job performs exactly one "
 1235:             f"conversion - the vertical datum shift - and the two fields "
 1236:             f"must state it together: Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY with "
 1237:             f"VerticalMode.VERTICAL. Any other mode either converts no "
 1238:             f"elevation or claims a horizontal conversion this direction "
 1239:             f"does not perform, so guessing which the caller meant would "
 1240:             f"decide what happens to every coordinate in the job."
 1241:         )
 1242:     if (
 1243:         mode is VerticalMode.VERTICAL
 1244:         and settings.direction is not Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
 1245:     ):
 1246:         raise ValueError(
 1247:             f"This job's vertical_mode is {VerticalMode.VERTICAL.value!r} "
 1248:             f"but its direction is {settings.direction.value!r}. "
 1249:             f"VerticalMode.VERTICAL converts elevations and nothing else, so "
 1250:             f"it pairs only with Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY; a job that also "
 1251:             f"converts coordinates states "
 1252:             f"VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL."
 1253:         )
 1254: 
 1255:     fields = (
 1256:         ("source_vertical_datum", settings.source_vertical_datum),
 1257:         ("target_vertical_datum", settings.target_vertical_datum),
 1258:     )
 1259:     for label, datum in fields:
 1260:         if datum is not None and not isinstance(datum, VerticalDatum):
 1261:             raise TypeError(
 1262:                 f"JobSettings.{label} must be a michspc.spc.vertical."
 1263:                 f"VerticalDatum record, or None to state that this job does "
 1264:                 f"not consult a vertical datum; got {type(datum).__name__} "
 1265:                 f"({datum!r}). Every record in this program's core carries "
 1266:                 f"code, name and citation, so a zone, a reference frame or a "
 1267:                 f"geoid model duck-types a long way before failing on an "
 1268:                 f"attribute nobody catches (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #11, "
 1269:                 f"finding 1). Pass michspc.spc.vertical.NGVD29 or NAVD88."
 1270:             )
 1271: 
 1272:     if mode is VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL:
 1273:         supplied = [label for label, datum in fields if datum is not None]
 1274:         if supplied:
 1275:             names = " and ".join(supplied)
 1276:             verb = "were" if len(supplied) > 1 else "was"
 1277:             raise ValueError(
 1278:                 f"This job is horizontal-only, but {names} {verb} supplied. "
 1279:                 f"A horizontal job never applies or records a vertical "
 1280:                 f"datum, so accepting one would let a caller believe an "
 1281:                 f"elevation conversion happened when none did - an "
 1282:                 f"unconverted height wearing a datum tag is exactly the "
 1283:                 f"ordinary-looking wrong number this program refuses. Set "
 1284:                 f"vertical_mode=VerticalMode.HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL to "
 1285:                 f"convert elevations, or state None for both datums."
 1286:             )
 1287:         return None
 1288: 
 1289:     missing = [label for label, datum in fields if datum is None]
 1290:     if missing:
 1291:         names = " and ".join(missing)
 1292:         verb = "were" if len(missing) > 1 else "was"
 1293:         raise ValueError(
 1294:             # mode.value spells the job: "horizontal and vertical" or
 1295:             # "vertical". One raise for both modes - the same code path on
 1296:             # purpose, so the two cannot come to refuse differently.
 1297:             f"A {mode.value} job needs both vertical datums, and "
 1298:             f"{names} {verb} not stated. Neither has a default: NGVD 29 and "
 1299:             f"NAVD 88 heights differ by up to 0.41 m across Michigan while "
 1300:             f"looking identical, so assuming a datum would silently relabel "
 1301:             f"every elevation in the job. Pass michspc.spc.vertical.NGVD29 "
 1302:             f"or NAVD88 for each."
 1303:         )
 1304: 
 1305:     return require_vertical_pair(
 1306:         settings.source_vertical_datum, settings.target_vertical_datum
 1307:     )

 1310: def _require_transformation_matches_settings(
 1311:     transformation: VerticalTransformation, settings: JobSettings
 1312: ) -> None:
 1313:     """The datum tag is CHECKED, not carried (DESIGN.md #36 reviewer note).
 1314: 
 1315:     ``apply_shift`` takes a bare float, so nothing downstream could notice a
 1316:     transformation looked up for one pair of datums being applied to a job
 1317:     that stated another - the shifted height would look entirely ordinary.
 1318:     ``run`` passes down the record ``require_vertical_pair`` returned for the
 1319:     settings' own datums; this holds that wiring at the point of use, by
 1320:     ``code``, the rule the registry itself resolves by. if/raise, never
 1321:     assert: the suite and the shipped program run under -O.
 1322:     """
 1323:     stated_source = settings.source_vertical_datum
 1324:     stated_target = settings.target_vertical_datum
 1325:     source_code = stated_source.code if stated_source is not None else None
 1326:     target_code = stated_target.code if stated_target is not None else None
 1327:     if (
 1328:         transformation.source.code != source_code
 1329:         or transformation.target.code != target_code
 1330:     ):
 1331:         raise ValueError(
 1332:             f"The vertical transformation applied to this row converts "
 1333:             f"{transformation.source.code} -> {transformation.target.code}, "
 1334:             f"but the job's settings state {source_code} -> {target_code}. "
 1335:             f"Applying it would move every elevation between datums nobody "
 1336:             f"chose, and nothing downstream could tell: the shift is a bare "
 1337:             f"number and the shifted height looks ordinary. The record must "
 1338:             f"be the one require_vertical_pair returned for the settings' "
 1339:             f"own datums."
 1340:         )
 1341:

 1343: def _require_geodetic_in_range(
 1344:     latitude: float,
 1345:     longitude: float,
 1346:     longitude_as_written: float,
 1347:     settings: JobSettings,
 1348:     context: str,
 1349: ) -> None:
 1350:     """Refuse a latitude or longitude no signed geodetic position can carry.
 1351: 
 1352:     Placed here - the single entry point where the file's longitude sign
 1353:     convention is applied - rather than left to the core, so the refusal can
 1354:     name the row, the value as the file wrote it, and the convention in
 1355:     force, none of which ``lambert._require_valid_geodetic`` (which guards
 1356:     the same domain and still runs after this) can see.
 1357: 
 1358:     Recorded against DESIGN.md #38's note to WP-V6: the NGS grid readers
 1359:     accept 0-360 east longitudes silently - ``to_east_longitude`` adjusts
 1360:     only negatives, so ``shift_m(43.0, 275.5)`` equals
 1361:     ``shift_m(43.0, -84.5)`` byte-identically. Through ``job.run`` the
 1362:     horizontal conversion's own domain gate already refused such a value
 1363:     before any grid was consulted; this refusal keeps that property stated
 1364:     at the boundary that owns it instead of inherited from the projection's
 1365:     internals, and says which ROW is wrong, which a file of thousands of
 1366:     points needs.
 1367: 
 1368:     The bounds are the core's exactly - latitude strictly inside (-90, 90),
 1369:     longitude within [-180, 180] - so no value can pass here and refuse
 1370:     there, or the reverse. NaN fails both comparisons and is refused too.
 1371:     """
 1372:     convention = settings.longitude_convention.value
 1373:     if not (-90.0 < latitude < 90.0):
 1374:         raise ValueError(
 1375:             f"{context}: the latitude column reads {latitude!r}, which is "
 1376:             f"not a geodetic latitude - it must lie strictly between -90 and "
 1377:             f"90 degrees. Check that the file's second column really holds "
 1378:             f"latitudes in decimal degrees, and that the latitude and "
 1379:             f"longitude columns are not swapped. Refused rather than "
 1380:             f"converted, because a coordinate computed from it would look "
 1381:             f"ordinary and be meaningless."
 1382:         )
 1383:     if not (-180.0 <= longitude <= 180.0):
 1384:         advice = (
 1385:             f"Subtract 360 from it ({longitude - 360.0:.6f} here), or export "
 1386:             f"the file again with signed longitudes."
 1387:             if 180.0 < longitude < 360.0
 1388:             else "Correct the file, or the convention selected for it."
 1389:         )
 1390:         raise ValueError(
 1391:             f"{context}: the longitude column reads {longitude_as_written!r}, "
 1392:             f"which under the '{convention}' convention this job states is a "
 1393:             f"signed longitude of {longitude!r} - outside the -180 to 180 "
 1394:             f"range a signed longitude can have. A value between 180 and 360 "
 1395:             f"is the 0-360 EAST convention, which this program deliberately "
 1396:             f"does not read from a file: 275.5 east and -84.5 both name the "
 1397:             f"same meridian, and the NGS grid files use 0-360 internally, so "
 1398:             f"an unconverted 0-360 longitude would look up plausible geoid "
 1399:             f"and VERTCON values while the State Plane conversion placed the "
 1400:             f"point thousands of kilometres away. " + advice
 1401:         )

  453: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  454: class EllipsoidHeightReading:
  455:     """The h -> H half of one converted point: the model, the separation it
  456:     read, and both heights.
  457: 
  458:     Recorded whenever an ellipsoid height was converted, in EVERY mode - the
  459:     horizontal modes convert it too, for the factors, even though the Z column
  460:     keeps the height as supplied (the owner's instruction). The arithmetic:
  461: 
  462:         H = h - N
  463: 
  464:     with N the geoid separation at the point's own position, negative
  465:     throughout Michigan, so H is about 34 m LARGER than h here. The datum of H
  466:     is the MODEL's, not anything the user typed - which is why the model's
  467:     datum code is carried on the record rather than derived later from
  468:     settings that may name a different one.
  469: 
  470:     ``__post_init__`` enforces the arithmetic, in ``GeoidSwapReading``'s
  471:     idiom: a record whose stated orthometric height disagrees with its own two
  472:     ingredients cannot be constructed, so a sign error cannot be recorded as
  473:     though it were intended.
  474:     """
  475: 
  476:     geoid_model_name: str
  477:     """The model that supplied N, e.g. "GEOID18"."""
  478: 
  479:     vertical_datum_code: str
  480:     """The model's own vertical datum - the datum H is in. NAVD88 for both
  481:     shipped models."""
  482: 
  483:     ellipsoid_height_m: float
  484:     """h, exactly as supplied, converted to metres. The smaller number."""
  485: 
  486:     geoid_height_m: float
  487:     """N at the point's pivot position, metres. Negative in Michigan."""
  488: 
  489:     orthometric_height_m: float
  490:     """H = h - N, metres. Enforced against its ingredients below."""
  491: 
  492:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
  493:         for name in ("geoid_model_name", "vertical_datum_code"):
  494:             value = getattr(self, name)
  495:             if not isinstance(value, str) or not value.strip():
  496:                 raise ValueError(
  497:                     f"EllipsoidHeightReading.{name} must be a non-empty "
  498:                     f"string naming what was used; got {value!r}."
  499:                 )
  500:         for name in (
  501:             "ellipsoid_height_m",
  502:             "geoid_height_m",
  503:             "orthometric_height_m",
  504:         ):
  505:             value = getattr(self, name)
  506:             if not isinstance(value, float) or not math.isfinite(value):
  507:                 raise ValueError(
  508:                     f"EllipsoidHeightReading.{name} must be a finite float in "
  509:                     f"metres; got {value!r}."
  510:                 )
  511:         expected = self.ellipsoid_height_m - self.geoid_height_m
  512:         if abs(self.orthometric_height_m - expected) > 1e-12:
  513:             raise ValueError(
  514:                 f"EllipsoidHeightReading states an orthometric height of "
  515:                 f"{self.orthometric_height_m!r} m, but its own ingredients "
  516:                 f"give H = h - N = {self.ellipsoid_height_m!r} - "
  517:                 f"{self.geoid_height_m!r} = {expected!r} m. The record must "
  518:                 f"agree with its own arithmetic; a height and a separation "
  519:                 f"are both bare floats, and adding where the rule subtracts "
  520:                 f"is wrong by twice the separation - about 68 m in Michigan."
  521:             )
  522: 
  523: 
  524: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  525: class GeoidSwapReading:
  526:     """The geoid-to-geoid half of one converted point: which two models, the
  527:     two separations read at the pivot, and the value that moved the height.
  528: 
  529:     Exists only on an IDENTITY vertical job whose two sides state different
  530:     geoid models (the owner's feature, 2026-08-09). The arithmetic it
  531:     records: the ellipsoid height h = H + N is a property of the point, not
  532:     of the model, so holding it fixed and changing N re-derives the
  533:     orthometric height under the output model:
  534: 
  535:         H_out = H_in + N_in - N_out
  536: 
  537:     ``shift_m`` is exactly ``n_source_m - n_target_m`` - the value ADDED to
  538:     the input height - and ``__post_init__`` enforces that arithmetic, so a
  539:     record whose stated shift disagrees with its own ingredients cannot be
  540:     constructed. The datum ``VerticalReading`` beside this one stays the
  541:     identity record: both are true - the datum did not change, the geoid did.
  542:     """
  543: 
  544:     source_model_name: str
  545:     """The input-side model's ``GeoidModel.name``, e.g. "GEOID12B"."""
  546: 
  547:     target_model_name: str
  548:     """The output-side model's name - the model the Z column is stated
  549:     against, and the model the factors were computed from."""
  550: 
  551:     n_source_m: float
  552:     """The input model's geoid separation at the pivot, metres. Negative
  553:     throughout Michigan."""
  554: 
  555:     n_target_m: float
  556:     """The output model's geoid separation at the same pivot, metres."""
  557: 
  558:     shift_m: float
  559:     """``n_source_m - n_target_m``, metres: what was ADDED to the input
  560:     height. Enforced against the two separations at construction."""
  561: 
  562:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
  563:         # if/raise, never assert: the suite and the shipped program run
  564:         # under -O, which strips asserts (docs/DESIGN.md section 7).
  565:         for label, name in (
  566:             ("source_model_name", self.source_model_name),
  567:             ("target_model_name", self.target_model_name),
  568:         ):
  569:             if not isinstance(name, str) or not name.strip():
  570:                 # The #11-finding-1 guard for this record's likeliest
  571:                 # impostor: a GeoidModel RECORD passed where its name
  572:                 # belongs would print "GeoidModel(name='GEOID18', ...)" into
  573:                 # a label a surveyor reads.
  574:                 raise TypeError(
  575:                     f"GeoidSwapReading.{label} must be the geoid model's own "
  576:                     f"non-empty name string; got {type(name).__name__} "
  577:                     f"({name!r}). Pass model.name, not the record."
  578:                 )
  579:         for label, value in (
  580:             ("n_source_m", self.n_source_m),
  581:             ("n_target_m", self.n_target_m),
  582:             ("shift_m", self.shift_m),
  583:         ):
  584:             if not isinstance(value, float) or not math.isfinite(value):
  585:                 raise ValueError(
  586:                     f"GeoidSwapReading.{label} must be a finite number of "
  587:                     f"metres; got {value!r}. A non-finite separation cannot "
  588:                     f"move a height."
  589:                 )
  590:         if self.source_model_name == self.target_model_name:
  591:             raise ValueError(
  592:                 f"GeoidSwapReading names {self.source_model_name!r} on both "
  593:                 f"sides. The same model on both sides is the ordinary "
  594:                 f"identity job - no swap step runs and no swap record "
  595:                 f"exists - so a record claiming one is a wiring defect."
  596:             )
  597:         if abs(self.shift_m - (self.n_source_m - self.n_target_m)) > 1e-12:
  598:             # The one place the swap arithmetic is defined; every surface
  599:             # reports shift_m, so a record that disagreed with its own
  600:             # ingredients would print a number nothing derived.
  601:             raise ValueError(
  602:                 f"GeoidSwapReading.shift_m ({self.shift_m!r}) does not equal "
  603:                 f"n_source_m - n_target_m "
  604:                 f"({self.n_source_m!r} - {self.n_target_m!r} = "
  605:                 f"{self.n_source_m - self.n_target_m!r}). The shift IS that "
  606:                 f"difference - H_out = H_in + N_in - N_out - and a record "
  607:                 f"carrying any other number is wrong by construction."
  608:             )
  609:

  611: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  612: class ConvertedPoint:
  613:     """One point, converted, with the evidence for it."""
  614: 
  615:     row: pnezd.PnezdRow
  616:     conversion: PointConversion
  617:     factors: Factors
  618: 
  619:     output_northing: float
  620:     output_easting: float
  621:     """In the OUTPUT unit, ready to format."""
  622: 
  623:     output_elevation: float | None
  624:     """In the output unit.
  625: 
  626:     For a HORIZONTAL job this is the input elevation re-expressed if the units
  627:     differ, and nothing else - orthometric height does not depend on the
  628:     horizontal zone. For a HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL job it is the SHIFTED
  629:     height, expressed in the target vertical datum ``vertical`` names:
  630:     docs/PLAN-vertical-datums.md section 3.6 explicitly repeals the "unchanged
  631:     by the conversion" sentence this field carried for the Z column. None when
  632:     the point carried no elevation - or when the vertical shift was
  633:     unavailable (``WarningCode.VERTICAL_SHIFT_UNAVAILABLE``), because an
  634:     unconverted height printed as converted is the tier sentence's failure
  635:     mode.
  636:     """
  637: 
  638:     vertical: VerticalReading | None = None
  639:     """The vertical transformation evidence for this point, or None.
  640: 
  641:     None on every point of a HORIZONTAL job, on a vertical job's point that
  642:     carried no elevation (there is no height to shift), and on a point whose
  643:     shift was unavailable (the warning beside it says so).
  644:     """
  645: 
  646:     ellipsoid_height: EllipsoidHeightReading | None = None
  647:     """The h -> H evidence for this point, or None.
  648: 
  649:     Present exactly when this point's height was supplied as an ellipsoid
  650:     height and the geoid tile covered its position - in ANY mode, including
  651:     horizontal, where the conversion still happens for the factors even
  652:     though the Z column keeps the ellipsoid height. None on every
  653:     orthometric-input point, on a point with no height at all, and on one
  654:     whose position the tile does not cover, where the warning says so.
  655:     """
  656: 
  657:     geoid_swap: GeoidSwapReading | None = None
  658:     """The geoid-to-geoid evidence for this point, or None.
  659: 
  660:     Present exactly when a geoid change moved this point's elevation: an
  661:     identity vertical job whose two sides state different geoid models, on a
  662:     point whose elevation was read and whose position both tiles cover. None
  663:     everywhere else - including a swap job's point whose elevation was
  664:     refused for coverage, where ``vertical`` is None too and the warning
  665:     says why.
  666:     """
  667: 
  668:     warnings: tuple[ConversionWarning, ...] = field(default_factory=tuple)
  669: 
  670:     @property
  671:     def point_id(self) -> str:
  672:         return self.row.point_id

 """Readable headings for the warning kinds this program currently raises.
 
 Presentation only. It does NOT decide which warnings are printed - see
 ``_warning_codes_present``. A heading missing from this table changes how a
 warning is introduced, never whether the surveyor is shown it.
 """
 
 
 def _warning_codes_present(result: JobResult) -> list:
     """Every warning code this job actually raised, in the order to print.
 
     Driven by the warnings, not by ``_WARNING_HEADINGS``. Iterating the heading
     table instead would mean a warning kind added later, before anyone wrote it
     a heading, was counted in the "N warning(s)" total at the top of the section
     and then never printed underneath it - the report telling the surveyor that
     something is wrong and declining to say what. Latent rather than live today,
     because both current codes have headings; structural, because the next code
     added would activate it silently.
 
     Known kinds keep their declared order so the section reads the same way from
     job to job; anything unheaded follows, in the order it first appeared.
     """
     raised = []
     for _point_id, warning in result.warnings:
         if warning.code not in raised:
             raised.append(warning.code)
 
     known = [code for code in _WARNING_HEADINGS if code in raised]
     return known + [code for code in raised if code not in _WARNING_HEADINGS]
 
 
 def _warning_heading(code) -> str:
     """A heading for a code, falling back to the code itself.
 
     The fallback is deliberately the raw code text rather than something
     generic like "OTHER": a surveyor reading an unfamiliar heading can search
     for it, and it is honest about being unpolished.
     """
     registered = _WARNING_HEADINGS.get(code)
     if registered is not None:
         return registered
     return str(getattr(code, "value", code)).replace("-", " ").replace("_", " ").upper()

     filed and believed.

+def _ellipsoid_height_method_block(settings, model_name) -> list[str]:
+    """The METHOD section's account of an ellipsoid-height input.
+
+    Facts only, in ``_geoid_swap_method_block``'s shape and under the same
+    standing instruction (the owner's, 2026-08-09, #33/#34/#45 extended to the
+    written record): the model with its tile filename and digest resolved from
+    the registry, the arithmetic, and the datum the derived heights are in.
+
+    The mode paragraph is the load-bearing one, because the two modes do
+    different things with the same conversion and the difference is invisible
+    in the numbers alone: horizontal mode writes the Z column back exactly as
+    supplied and uses the derived height only for the factors, while the
+    vertical modes write the derived height. A reader holding only the clean
+    export needs the record to say which of those produced it.
+    """
+    record = geoid_model_by_name(model_name)
+    lines: list[str] = []
+    add = lines.append
+
+    add("ELLIPSOID HEIGHT CONVERSION")
+    add("")
+    add(
+        f"Input heights      stated as ELLIPSOID heights (above the GRS 80 "
+        f"ellipsoid),"
+    )
+    add("                   as a GNSS receiver produces them.")
+    add(
+        f"Geoid model        {record.name}, NGS grid tile "
+        f"{record.tile_filename}"
+    )
+    add(f"                   SHA-256 {record.sha256}")
+    lines.extend(
+        _labelled_paragraph(
+            "Arithmetic",
+            f"The orthometric height is derived at each point's own "
+            f"horizontal position: H = h - N, where h is the height the Z "
+            f"column supplied and N is the {record.name} geoid height there, "
+            f"in metres. N is negative throughout Michigan, so H is the "
+            f"LARGER number - about 34 m larger. The derived heights are in "
+            f"{record.vertical_datum.code}, the datum {record.name} publishes "
+            f"separations for; an ellipsoid height is in no vertical datum "
+            f"itself.",
+        )
+    )
+    if settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations:
+        lines.extend(
+            _labelled_paragraph(
+                "This job",
+                "converts the elevations, so the Z column of every export "
+                "carries the derived orthometric height, after any vertical "
+                "datum shift stated below.",
+            )
+        )

 def _geoid_swap_method_block(swap_models) -> list[str]:
     """The METHOD section's account of a geoid-to-geoid conversion.
 
     Facts only, stated plainly (the owner's instruction, 2026-08-09, the
     #33/#34/#45 ruling extended to the written record): the two models with
     their tile filenames and digests resolved from the registry - the same
     records the loader authenticates against - and the arithmetic. No
     caveat prose and no uncertainty lecture; the sigma surfaces simply read
     N/A.
     """
     source_record = geoid_model_by_name(swap_models[0].name)
     target_record = geoid_model_by_name(swap_models[1].name)
     lines: list[str] = []
     add = lines.append
 
     add("GEOID CHANGE")
     add("")
     add(
         f"Input geoid        {source_record.name}, NGS grid tile "
         f"{source_record.tile_filename}"
     )
     add(f"                   SHA-256 {source_record.sha256}")
     add(
         f"Output geoid       {target_record.name}, NGS grid tile "
         f"{target_record.tile_filename}"
     )
     add(f"                   SHA-256 {target_record.sha256}")
     lines.extend(
         _labelled_paragraph(
             "Arithmetic",
             f"The ellipsoid height is held fixed and the orthometric height "
             f"re-derived under the output model: H_out = H_in + N_in - "
             f"N_out, where N_in and N_out are the {source_record.name} and "
             f"{target_record.name} geoid heights at the point's horizontal "
             f"position, in metres. The Z column carries H_out; the _full.csv "
             f"export's Vertical shift column carries N_in - N_out per "
             f"point.",
         )
     )
     add("")
     return lines

 def build_report(result: JobResult) -> str:
     """Render the job record."""
     settings = result.settings
     if settings.input_path is None or settings.output_directory is None:
         # The record's INPUT block opens with "File" and its OUTPUT block opens
         # with "Folder". Neither line can be written from None, and neither may
         # be filled in with a plausible substitute: this record is what a sealed
         # survey is defended with, so a file name it never read or a folder it
         # never wrote to would be a falsehood in the one document that exists to
         # say what happened (docs/DESIGN.md section 1, amendment #26).
         # Name which one is actually missing. Saying "neither" when only one is
         # absent is a false diagnostic about the half-pathless state, which is
         # a state this program explicitly supports (closing review gate).
         missing = []
         if settings.input_path is None:
             missing.append("no input file")
         if settings.output_directory is None:
             missing.append("no output folder")
         raise ValueError(
             f"A job record names the input file it read and the folder it "
             f"wrote to, and this job states it had {' and '.join(missing)} "
             f"(input_path={settings.input_path!r}, "
             f"output_directory={settings.output_directory!r}). A job with no "
             f"file, such as a single typed point, is displayed rather than "
             f"recorded."
         )
     generated = datetime.now(timezone.utc).astimezone()
 
     # The vertical disclosure switch (WP-V7). Everything it gates below is
     # ADDITIVE and appears only on a job that converts elevations
     # (HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL, or vertical-only): a horizontal job's record
     # is byte-identical to what this program has always written, which is the
     # WP-V6 gate's hard constraint on this package. The transformation is
     # resolved through the same registry lookup job.run performed, so the
     # record and the computation quote one record.
     vertical = settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations
     transformation = (
         require_vertical_pair(
             settings.source_vertical_datum, settings.target_vertical_datum
         )
         if vertical
         else None
     )
     # (input model, output model) when this job converts BETWEEN geoid
     # models, else None - job.geoid_swap_models' one rule, so the record and
     # the computation cannot disagree about whether a swap ran. Every block
     # this gates is additive and swap-only: a job without a swap keeps every
     # byte it wrote before the feature.
     swap_models = geoid_swap_models(settings, transformation)
+    # The model that derived H from h, or None when the Z column already held
+    # elevations. Resolved through the same job-layer rule the computation
+    # used, so the record cannot name a model the conversion did not read.

+        factors_geoid_model(settings, transformation).name
+        if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID
+        else None

     add(_RULE)
     add("")
     add(f"Generated          {generated.strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S %Z')}")
     add(f"Software           {APP_NAME} version {__version__}")
     add("")
 
     # ---------------------------------------------------------------- input
     add(_THIN)
     add("INPUT")
     add(_THIN)
     add(f"File               {settings.input_path}")
     if result.input_sha256:
         add(f"SHA-256            {result.input_sha256}")
     else:
         # No digest means no bytes passed through this program: the rows were
         # handed to the job already parsed. Saying so is the only honest line
         # available, and it is a great deal more honest than the hash of
         # whatever happens to sit at the path above - which is what this line
         # used to print, certifying a file that was never converted
         # (WP-R3 fix 2). The file name is left in place because it is what the
         # caller stated; the sentence below says what it is worth.
         add("SHA-256            not available")
         add("                   These coordinates were supplied to this program")
         add("                   already parsed, so it never read the file named")
         add("                   above and nothing here certifies its contents.")
     add(f"Coordinate rows    {result.input_row_count}")
     if result.skipped_blank_lines:
         add(f"Blank lines        {result.skipped_blank_lines} (skipped)")
     lines.extend(_input_format_block(settings))
     if vertical:
         # Which surface the file's Z column measures from. Stated with the
         # datum's full name: NGVD 29 and NAVD 88 heights differ by up to

         # is a token a reader six months later may not place.
         source_datum = settings.source_vertical_datum
         add(f"Vertical datum     {source_datum.name} ({source_datum.code})")
     add("")
 
     # --------------------------------------------------------------- output
     add(_THIN)
     add("OUTPUT")
     add(_THIN)
     add(f"Folder             {settings.output_directory}")
     add(f"Conversion         {settings.direction.value}")
     if settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY:

     if vertical:

 """The job's output: one ZIP archive containing three files.
 
 1. ``<name>_<zone>.csv``       - clean PNEZD, for import straight into CAD
 2. ``<name>_<zone>_full.csv``  - every computed quantity, for the record
 3. ``<name>_<zone>_README.txt``- the job record explaining both (report.py)
 
 **The archive is the only deliverable.** Nothing is written loose beside it
 (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #17). The three files travel together or not at all,
 so a PNEZD export cannot be filed or emailed without the record explaining how
 it was derived - which matters for a file that ends up supporting a sealed
 survey. The cost is that importing into CAD means unzipping first; that was the
 owner's explicit trade.
 
 The clean export carries nothing but the five PNEZD fields. Warning flags
 and scale factors live in the other two, because a CAD import
 that meets an unexpected sixth column either fails or silently shifts
 everything one field left.
 """
 
 from __future__ import annotations

 _PNEZD_HEADERLESS = True
 """The clean export has no header row, matching the input format exactly.
 
 A header would make the output un-round-trippable through this program's own
 reader, which is the property `verify_round_trip` below exists to guarantee.
 """
 
 
 def _geodetic_coordinate_columns(settings) -> bool:
     """Whether this job's OUTPUT coordinate columns hold degrees.
 
     True for a State-Plane-to-geodetic job, and for a vertical-only job whose
     input is geodetic - that job's exports mirror the input's own layout, so
     a geodetic input keeps geodetic columns. Stated once, here, because the
     clean export, the audit CSV and the round-trip verifier must all take
     the same branch or the verifier would check a file that was not written.
     """
     return settings.direction is Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC or (
         settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
         and settings.source_zone is None
     )
 
 
 def _geodetic_source_columns(settings) -> bool:
     """Whether this job's INPUT file's columns two and three hold degrees."""
     return settings.direction is Direction.GEODETIC_TO_ZONE or (
         settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
         and settings.source_zone is None
     )
 
 
 def output_stem(result: JobResult) -> str:
     """``<input stem>_<zone abbreviation>``, e.g. ``24-118-topo_MI-C``."""
     settings = result.settings
     if settings.input_path is None:
         # The stem of every member of the archive is built from the input file's
         # name, so a job that came from no file cannot be named. Refused rather
         # than substituting something like "typed" or "untitled": the members
         # would then claim to describe a file that does not exist, in the one
         # deliverable a sealed survey is supported by (amendment #26).
         raise WriteError(
             "This job states it came from no input file "
             "(JobSettings.input_path is None), so its export cannot be named - "
             "every file inside the archive is named after the input file. A "
             "job with no file, such as a single typed point, is displayed "
             "rather than written."
         )
     if settings.direction is Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC:
         suffix = "GEODETIC"
     elif settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY:
         # No target zone exists in this mode, so no abbreviation could name
         # the export; what happened to the file is the vertical conversion.
         suffix = "VERTICAL"
     else:
         suffix = settings.target_zone.abbrev
     return f"{settings.input_path.stem}_{suffix}"
 
 
 def clean_pnezd_rows(result: JobResult) -> list[list[str]]:
     """The CAD-bound export: point, northing, easting, elevation, description."""
     settings = result.settings
     rows: list[list[str]] = []
 
     for point in result.points:
         if _geodetic_coordinate_columns(settings):
             # For a vertical-only geodetic job these are the INPUT positions
             # re-rendered through the standard formatters - the longitude
             # exactly as the file wrote it, in its own convention - so the
             # export mirrors the import, formatting-normalized.
             northing = fmt.latitude(point.output_northing)
             easting = fmt.longitude(point.output_easting)
             # The OUTPUT unit, exactly as in every other direction. The columns
             # either side of it are degrees, but the Z column is still linear
             # and the job record's "Units out" and "Precision written" lines
             # both describe it in the output unit (WP-R2 fix A).
             elevation = fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, settings.output_unit)
         else:
             northing = fmt.coordinate(point.output_northing, settings.output_unit)
             easting = fmt.coordinate(point.output_easting, settings.output_unit)

 def vertical_sigma_heading(unit) -> str:
     """``Shift sigma (ift)`` - the sigma column heading, same rule as the
     shift's: the job's input unit, one wording on every surface (#17), the
     cell converted by the same unit object."""
     return f"Shift sigma ({unit.code})"

 def audit_columns(result: JobResult) -> list[str]:
     """The audit CSV's header row for this job's direction.
 
     Identical to ``AUDIT_COLUMNS`` except where one end of the job is geodetic,
     in which case that end's pair of columns is renamed to say what it actually
     holds. The other end's headings never move.
 
     A job that converts elevations (``VerticalMode.converts_elevations`` -
     HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL, and vertical-only) additionally carries six
     columns a horizontal CSV must never grow (WP-V7,
     docs/PLAN-vertical-datums.md section 5.2):
 
     * ``Source vertical datum`` / ``Target vertical datum`` - which surface
       each end's heights are expressed in, per row, so a row cut out of this
       file still says what its two heights mean.
     * ``Source elevation (<input unit>)`` - the PRE-shift height as the file
       supplied it, so this file answers "how was this Z derived" without
       re-running anything. The existing ``Elevation`` column keeps the TARGET
       height, which is what the clean export carries.
     * ``Vertical shift (<input unit>)`` - the modeled shift applied,
       converted into the job's input unit (the owner's instruction,
       2026-08-09 - the unit the elevations were supplied in; in vertical-only
       mode input and output units are equal by construction, and in
       Horizontal + Vertical the input unit still governs these two columns
       while the Elevation column stays in the output unit as always). 0.0000
       for an identity, which really is a zero shift, not an absence.
     * ``Shift sigma (<input unit>)`` - its one-sigma uncertainty, same unit
       as the shift it qualifies, and ``formatting.NOT_AVAILABLE`` where none
       can be stated (an identity ran no model; where the error model
       interpolates below zero there is no physical sigma) - NEVER a number in
       either case (docs/DESIGN.md #36).
     * ``Geoid model`` - which model's separations the factor columns were
       computed from. Two shipped models now differ by up to 32 mm at one
       Michigan anchor (DESIGN.md #40 LOW 5), so a vertical CSV names its own.
       A HORIZONTAL CSV deliberately does NOT gain this column: its layout is
       the status quo since 0.1.0, relied on by downstream spreadsheets, and
       the job record inside the same ZIP names the model (#17) - that
       standing mitigation stays the horizontal answer.
     """
     columns = list(AUDIT_COLUMNS)
     settings = result.settings
     if _geodetic_source_columns(settings):
         columns[2], columns[3] = SOURCE_COLUMNS_GEODETIC
     if _geodetic_coordinate_columns(settings):
         columns[5], columns[6] = TARGET_COLUMNS_GEODETIC
+    if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID:
+        # One column, present exactly when the job stated ellipsoid input, so
+        # no pre-existing job's layout moves by a byte. It earns its place on
+        # a HORIZONTAL job especially: there the Elevation column holds the
+        # ellipsoid height itself, passed through, and a row cut out of this
+        # file and pasted elsewhere would otherwise carry no statement at all
+        # of what kind of height it is - the same reasoning that put "Source
+        # vertical datum" on every row of a vertical job.
+        columns.insert(columns.index("Elevation") + 1, "Input height kind")
+
     if settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations:
         # The vertical block sits directly after Elevation, so the target
         # height and the ingredients it was derived from read side by side;
         # Geoid model sits directly before the geoid height it governs.
-        anchor = columns.index("Elevation") + 1
-        columns[anchor:anchor] = [
+        anchor = (
+            columns.index("Input height kind") + 1
+            if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID
+            else columns.index("Elevation") + 1
+        )
+        vertical_block = [
             "Source vertical datum",
             "Target vertical datum",
             f"Source elevation ({settings.input_unit.code})",
             vertical_shift_heading(settings.input_unit),
             vertical_sigma_heading(settings.input_unit),
         ]
+        if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID:
+            # The height the file actually supplied, in its own column, so
+            # "Source elevation" can go on meaning what it has always meant -
+            # the PRE-SHIFT orthometric height - and the row's arithmetic
+            # stays closed: Source elevation + Vertical shift = Elevation.
+            # Without it one of those two facts has to give.
+            vertical_block.insert(
+                2, f"Ellipsoid height in ({settings.input_unit.code})"
+            )
+        columns[anchor:anchor] = vertical_block
         if settings.source_geoid_model is not None:
             # The per-side feature's one new column, present exactly when
             # the job STATED an input-side model: which geoid model the
             # source elevations were stated against, beside the existing
             # "Geoid model" (which keeps naming the factors/output side).
             # Every job shape that predates the field - horizontal, and
             # every #41-era vertical shape - states None here and its CSV
             # layout does not change by a byte.
             columns.insert(columns.index("Geoid height (m)"), "Source geoid model")
         columns.insert(columns.index("Geoid height (m)"), "Geoid model")
     return columns
 
 
 def audit_rows(result: JobResult) -> list[list[str]]:
     """Every computed quantity for every point, with a header row.
 
     This is the file that answers "how was this coordinate derived" without
     anyone having to re-run the program.
     """
     settings = result.settings
     out_unit = settings.output_unit
     in_unit = settings.input_unit
     geodetic_source = _geodetic_source_columns(settings)
     geodetic_target = _geodetic_coordinate_columns(settings)
     vertical = settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations
 
     header = audit_columns(result)
     rows: list[list[str]] = [header]
 
     for point in result.points:
         conversion = point.conversion
         factors = point.factors
 
         # The source columns hold whatever the file's columns two and three
         # held. For a geodetic input that is a latitude and a longitude in
         # decimal degrees, and formatting them as linear coordinates rounded
         # them to the unit's 3 places: 42.73250000 was recorded as 42.733,
         # about 55 m of latitude, and -84.55550000 as -84.555, about 37 m of
         # longitude - in the one file that exists to say how the number was
         # derived (WP-R2 fix F). The longitude is printed as the file wrote it,
         # in the job's own convention; the signed pivot has its own column.
         if geodetic_source:
             source_northing = fmt.latitude(point.row.northing)
             source_easting = fmt.longitude(point.row.easting)
         else:
             source_northing = fmt.coordinate(point.row.northing, in_unit)
             source_easting = fmt.coordinate(point.row.easting, in_unit)
 
         # What the "Target zone" cell says the output system is. A
         # vertical-only job HAS no output horizontal system - the coordinate
         # columns beside this cell reproduce the input's - so the honest
         # statement is the direction itself: "vertical only".
         if settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY:
             target_system = settings.direction.value
         elif settings.target_zone:
             target_system = settings.target_zone.name
         else:
             target_system = "geodetic"
 
         row = (
             [
                 point.point_id,
                 settings.source_zone.name if settings.source_zone else "",
                 source_northing,
                 source_easting,
                 target_system,
                 fmt.coordinate(point.output_northing, out_unit)
                 if not geodetic_target
                 else fmt.latitude(point.output_northing),
                 fmt.coordinate(point.output_easting, out_unit)
                 if not geodetic_target
                 else fmt.longitude(point.output_easting),
                 fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, out_unit),
                 f"in {in_unit.code}, out {out_unit.code}",
                 fmt.latitude(conversion.latitude),
                 fmt.longitude(conversion.longitude),
                 fmt.geoid_height(factors.geoid_height),
                 fmt.geoid_height(factors.ellipsoid_height),
                 fmt.factor(conversion.source_scale_factor),
                 fmt.angle_dms(conversion.source_convergence),
                 fmt.factor(factors.grid_scale_factor),
                 fmt.angle_dms(conversion.target_convergence),
                 fmt.factor(factors.elevation_factor),
                 fmt.factor(factors.combined_factor),
                 fmt.signed_parts_per_million(factors.combined_factor),
                 "; ".join(w.code.value for w in point.warnings),
                 point.row.description,
             ]
         )
 
+        if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID:
+            row.insert(
+                header.index("Input height kind"), settings.input_height_kind.value
+            )
+
         if vertical:
             # Inserted at the vertical header's own indexes, computed from the
             # header this function just built, so the cells cannot land under
             # the wrong headings if either insertion point moves. The vertical
             # block first (it sits earlier), then Geoid model.
             # Which numbers the shift and sigma cells carry is stated once,
             # in vertical_shift_and_sigma_m, and shared with the on-screen
             # table: the datum reading's pair ordinarily, the SWAP shift
             # with no sigma on a geoid-to-geoid point, neither where no
             # reading exists (no elevation, or a coverage refusal - the
             # warnings cell says which). An identity reading carries
             # shift_m=0.0 - a real zero, printed as one; sigma_m is None on
             # an identity (no model ran) and where the error model
             # interpolates below zero (DESIGN.md #36) - both render N/A
             # through the formatter, never the raw figure.
             shift_m, sigma_m = vertical_shift_and_sigma_m(point)
             insert_at = header.index("Source vertical datum")
-            row[insert_at:insert_at] = [
+            # The PRE-shift ORTHOMETRIC height. For an orthometric-input job
+            # that is exactly what the file supplied; for an ellipsoid-input
+            # job it is the height DERIVED from it, because this column means
+            # "what the vertical shift was applied to" and the shift was
+            # applied to H, never to h. N/A where no height was derived - a
+            # blank Z, or a point off the geoid tile.
+            source_elevation = point.row.elevation
+            if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID:
+                source_elevation = (
+                    in_unit.from_meters(point.ellipsoid_height.orthometric_height_m)
+                    if point.ellipsoid_height is not None
+                    else None
+                )
+            vertical_cells = [
                 settings.source_vertical_datum.code,
                 settings.target_vertical_datum.code,
-                # The PRE-shift height exactly as the file supplied it, in the
-                # input unit - N/A for a blank Z field, exactly as the target
-                # Elevation cell is.
-                fmt.coordinate(point.row.elevation, in_unit),
+                fmt.coordinate(source_elevation, in_unit),
                 # Both cells are converted into IN_UNIT - the same unit
                 # object the two headings above were built from, so heading
                 # and value cannot claim different units.
                 fmt.vertical_quantity(shift_m, in_unit),
                 fmt.vertical_quantity(sigma_m, in_unit),

+                vertical_cells.insert(

                 row.insert(

                 header.index("Geoid model"),

                 # is applied - in which case there is no model to name and
                 # the factor columns beside it are N/A too.

                 if result.geoid_model is not None
                 else fmt.NOT_AVAILABLE,

     return rows
 
 
 def _expected_coordinates(result: JobResult, point) -> tuple[str, float, str, float]:
     """What the export's columns two and three must carry for this point.
 
     Returns ``(name, value, name, value)`` - the label the round-trip refusal
     uses for each column, and the number the job computed for it. Reads the
     same branch ``clean_pnezd_rows`` writes, so the two cannot describe
     different columns.
     """
     if _geodetic_coordinate_columns(result.settings):
         return ("latitude", point.output_northing, "longitude", point.output_easting)
     return ("northing", point.output_northing, "easting", point.output_easting)
 
 
 def _written_decimals(result: JobResult, geodetic: bool) -> int:
     """The number of decimal places the column in question was written to.
 
     Degrees are written to 8 places by ``formatting.latitude`` and
     ``formatting.longitude``; a linear column is written to its unit's own
     declared precision. The round-trip tolerance below is derived from this and
     from nothing else.
     """
     return 8 if geodetic else result.settings.output_unit.decimals
 
 
 # _rounding_tolerance stood here until the vertical-only gate (DESIGN.md #46,
 # MEDIUM 2): verify_round_trip now compares the re-read value EXACTLY against
 # the value the writer promised - the job's number rendered at the written
 # precision - rather than the pre-rounding float within half a place. The
 # tolerance form carried a trap: a value whose next decimal is exactly 5
 # rounds a hair past half a place, and in vertical-only mode the value is the
 # user's own literal, so ordinary metre northings refused the whole archive.
 # The exact form is strictly tighter and keeps every real failure.
 
 
 def verify_round_trip(rows: list[list[str]], result: JobResult) -> None:
     """Refuse to write a PNEZD file this program's own reader would reject.
 
     METHOD.md section 5: "a writer that refuses to produce a file its own
     reader would reject". The rows are rendered, re-parsed, and checked field
     for field against what the job computed, before anything reaches its final
     name.
 
     This has real teeth: a description containing a comma, an identifier
     containing a quote, or a value formatted as "nan" would all produce a file
     that looks written and imports wrongly.
 
     **Every field the reader returns is compared, not only the identifier.**
     It used to check ``point_id`` alone, and the reviewer demonstrated the
     consequence by replacing the whole expected row
     ``101,449212.689,13072628.343,N/A,IP`` with
     ``101,999999.999,888888.888,777.777,WRONG DESCRIPTION``: every coordinate,
     the elevation and the description were corrupted and the program's own
     safety gate passed the file (WP-R3 fix 1).
 
     **Compared against the reader's semantics, not against raw text.** The
     reader trims surrounding whitespace and treats a blank field, "N/A" and an
     exact 0.00 alike as "no elevation recorded", so the comparison is made
     against what it returns - a float, or None - rather than against the
     characters in the cell. The numeric comparison is EXACT against the value
