     """
     assert selftest._zone_to_zone_tolerance_ift() == pytest.approx(
         0.0136233595800524, abs=1e-15
     )
 
 
 # --------------------------------------------------------------------------
 # It passes from source, through the entry point the executable uses.
 # --------------------------------------------------------------------------
 
 
 def test_the_check_registry_holds_every_check_by_name():
     """A deleted CHECKS entry must fail a test, not merely shrink a tuple.
 
     Both tests that touch CHECKS are self-referential - they count or iterate
     whatever the tuple holds - so removing any single check left the suite
     green while the release notes went on claiming the bundle runs it
     (closing gate, MEDIUM 2; the DESIGN.md #38 finding-2 discipline, applied
     to this registry). The full ordered list is pinned: adding a check
     updates this list consciously, deleting one fails it. Falsified by
     removing the vertical-conversion entry: this test alone fails.
     """
     assert [name for name, _check in selftest.CHECKS] == [
         "version and application name",
         "bundled GEOID18 grid",
         "bundled VERTCON 3.0 grid pair",
         "vertical conversion against NGS NCAT",
         "bundled GEOID12B tile",
+        "ellipsoid height conversion",
         "lazily imported dependencies",
         "Qt startup and bundled icon",
         "end-to-end conversion against NGS NCAT",
     ]
 
 
 def test_every_check_passes_from_source():
     """The whole self-test, in process, with its transcript captured."""
     lines: list[str] = []
     selftest.run_selftest(lines.append)
 
     passed = [line for line in lines if line.startswith("PASS")]
     assert len(passed) == len(selftest.CHECKS)
     assert not [line for line in lines if line.startswith("FAIL")]
 
 
 def test_the_launcher_dispatches_selftest_and_reports_it(tmp_path):
     """``launch.py --selftest`` - the exact command line the build gate runs.
 
     Driven through ``launch.main`` rather than by re-implementing the dispatch,
     because the thing being checked IS the dispatch. The report file is checked
     too: a windowed bundle has no console, so the report is the only place a
     failure could be read from (michspc/selftest.py).
     """
     import launch
 
     report = tmp_path / "nested" / "selftest.txt"
     exit_code = launch.main(["--selftest", "--report", str(report)])
 
     assert exit_code == 0

    1: """NGS published Michigan benchmarks carrying BOTH heights. Frozen anchors.
    2: 
    3: Every value below was captured verbatim from the National Geodetic Survey's
    4: own benchmark service:
    5: 
    6:     https://geodesy.noaa.gov/api/nde/radial?lat=<lat>&lon=<lon>&radius=6&type=BM
    7: 
    8: Captured 2026-08-11. The raw JSON and the capture harness are committed at
    9: ``review/wp-e1-ellipsoid/``. **No test touches the network.**
   10: 
   11: Every mark here is a published NGS control point whose datasheet states an
   12: **NAVD 88 orthometric height** and a **GEOID18 geoid height** for the same
   13: position. That pairing is what makes them useful to the ellipsoid-height
   14: feature: NGS's own ellipsoid height at the mark is
   15: 
   16:     h = H + N
   17: 
   18: so a program told ``h`` must give back ``H``.
   19: 
   20: **What these anchors do and do not prove.** They are NOT an independent
   21: derivation of the geoid â€” NGS computed the published N from GEOID18, the same
   22: model this program reads, so the two cannot disagree about the model itself.
   23: What they exercise is the whole path at REAL PUBLISHED CONTROL rather than at
   24: arbitrary sampled positions: the reader's interpolation at fourteen marks
   25: spread over the entire state, in every zone, and the h -> H arithmetic on top
   26: of it. The independent check of the geoid model is elsewhere in the suite â€”
   27: ``geoid_anchors.py`` and ``geoid_discriminating_anchors.py``, captured from
   28: NGS's geoid service at off-node positions chosen to test interpolation.
   29: 
   30: **Why the tolerance is what it is, derived not chosen.** The input height is
   31: built from the two published figures, so
   32: 
   33:     H_returned = h - N_ours = (H_pub + N_pub) - N_ours
   34:                = H_pub + (N_pub - N_ours)
   35: 
   36: The published orthometric height's own precision cancels exactly, and the only
   37: error left is the separation disagreement. Measured at capture time over all
   38: fourteen marks, the worst was **0.75 mm** â€” and NGS prints N to 0.001 m, which
   39: carries +/-0.0005 m of quantization on its own before the reader's sub-
   40: millimetre residual (DESIGN.md #37) is counted. 0.0015 m is that bound.
   41: 
   42: Note the sign on every separation: negative, between about -33 and -37 m. In
   43: the conterminous United States the ellipsoid lies ABOVE the geoid, so an
   44: ellipsoid height is the SMALLER number and ``H = h - N`` makes a height
   45: larger. A conversion that made a Michigan height smaller has the sign wrong.
   46: """
   47: 
   48: from __future__ import annotations

   64:     longitude: float

   71:     """GEOID18 GEOID HEIGHT, metres. Negative throughout Michigan."""

   74:     def ellipsoid_height_m(self) -> float:
   75:         """h = H + N â€” NGS's own pairing, and the INPUT a test supplies.
   76: 
   77:         Derived rather than stored: storing it would be a third figure that
   78:         could drift out of step with the two it comes from, and this project
   79:         does not store a derived value beside its ingredients.

   81:         return self.orthometric_height_m + self.geoid_height_m

 succeeded in 563ms:
    1: """Ellipsoid-height input through ``job.run`` (WP-E2).
    2: 
    3: The mode decides ONE thing and one thing only: what lands in the Z column.
    4: Horizontal mode writes the height back exactly as supplied (the owner's
    5: instruction, 2026-08-11); the two vertical modes write the orthometric height.
    6: In BOTH the factors are computed from the orthometric height, because the
    7: elevation factor is R / (R + H + N) and a height that already contains the
    8: separation must not have it added a second time.
    9: 
   10: The anchors these pins sit on are in ``tests/test_ellipsoid_height.py`` and
   11: ``tests/fixtures/ellipsoid_height_anchors.py``. At the Houghton anchor NGS
   12: prints N18 = -33.796 m, so a 200.000 m NAVD 88 point has an ellipsoid height
   13: of 166.204 m, and converting that back must land on 200.000 m.
   14: """

   17: 
   18: import pytest
   19: 
   20: from michspc.fileio import formatting as fmt, geoid, pnezd

   22:     Direction,
   23:     EllipsoidHeightReading,

   28: )

   32: from michspc.spc.zones import MI_NORTH
   33: from tests.fixtures.geoid_anchors import GEOID_ANCHORS

   36: HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE = -88.5694
   37: N18_FIXTURE = next(
   38:     a.geoid_height_m
   39:     for a in GEOID_ANCHORS
   40:     if a.latitude == HOUGHTON_LATITUDE and a.longitude == HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE
   41: )
   42:

   47: # 0.001 m plus the reader's measured sub-millimetre residual (DESIGN.md #37).

   92:     return pnezd.parse_lines(
   93:         [

  109: def test_a_vertical_job_converts_the_gnss_height_to_the_published_elevation():
  110:     """The flagship. 166.204 m ellipsoid at Houghton becomes 200.000 m NAVD 88
  111:     â€” DESIGN.md #50's own anchor, read the other way."""
  112:     point = run(

  120:     # It went UP by the separation. A sign error lands 68 m away.

  122:

  124:     assert reading is not None

  127:     assert reading.ellipsoid_height_m == pytest.approx(HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M, abs=1e-9)
  128:     # Exact against the shipped grid, not the fixture: the record must agree
  129:     # with the grid it actually read.
  130:     assert reading.geoid_height_m == geoid.geoid_height(
  131:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE,
  132:         HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE,
  133:         geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL),
  134:     )
  135:     assert reading.orthometric_height_m == pytest.approx(
  136:         reading.ellipsoid_height_m - reading.geoid_height_m, abs=1e-12
  137:     )

  139: 
  140: def test_the_identity_branch_cannot_overwrite_the_converted_height():
  141:     """The design review's catch, pinned.
  142: 
  143:     The h -> H step rebinds ``elevation_m`` rather than assigning only
  144:     ``height_m``, because the identity branch a few lines below re-reads
  145:     ``elevation_m`` through ``apply_shift``. Assigning only ``height_m`` left
  146:     the flagship same-datum job writing the raw ellipsoid height while
  147:     reporting a conversion â€” the feature doing nothing at all, on the job it
  148:     exists for. This asserts the Z actually MOVED, which is what that seeding
  149:     fails.
  150:     """
  151:     point = run(
  152:         _vertical(input_height_kind=HeightKind.ELLIPSOID),
  153:         source=_at_houghton(HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M),
  154:     ).points[0]
  155: 
  156:     # The identity shift really did run, and really did shift by zero...
  157:     assert point.vertical is not None
  158:     assert point.vertical.shift_m == 0.0
  159:     # ...and yet the Z is not the supplied height: h -> H ran before it.
  160:     assert point.output_elevation - HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M == pytest.approx(
  161:         -N18_FIXTURE, abs=ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M
  162:     )
  163: 
  164: 
  165: def test_a_modeled_shift_takes_the_derived_elevation_not_the_gnss_height():
  166:     """NAVD 88 -> NGVD 29 from GNSS: h -> H first, VERTCON second.
  167: 
  168:     The order is the whole safety of it. VERTCON transforms an orthometric
  169:     height; handing it an ellipsoid height would shift a number 34 m away from
  170:     the one its grid was built for, and nothing downstream could notice.
  171:     """
  172:     point = run(
  173:         _vertical(
  174:             input_height_kind=HeightKind.ELLIPSOID, target_vertical_datum=NGVD29
  175:         ),
  176:         source=_at_houghton(HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M),
  177:     ).points[0]
  178: 
  179:     reading = point.ellipsoid_height
  180:     assert reading is not None
  181:     assert reading.orthometric_height_m == pytest.approx(
  182:         HOUGHTON_H_M, abs=ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M
  183:     )
  184:     # The Z is the derived elevation plus the modeled shift, not h plus it.
  185:     assert point.output_elevation == pytest.approx(
  186:         reading.orthometric_height_m + point.vertical.shift_m, abs=1e-9
  187:     )
  188:     # And #41's source-era factors used H, never h.
  189:     assert point.factors.orthometric_height == pytest.approx(
  190:         HOUGHTON_H_M, abs=ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M
  191:     )
  192: 
  193: 
  194: @pytest.mark.parametrize("unit", [METERS, INTERNATIONAL_FEET])
  195: def test_the_conversion_is_the_same_height_in_every_unit(unit):
  196:     # Written to four decimals in the job's own unit, then compared against
  197:     # THAT number rather than the unrounded one: in international feet the
  198:     # fourth decimal is 30 micrometres, and comparing to the unrounded value
  199:     # would be testing the test's formatting, not the program.
  200:     written = f"{unit.from_meters(HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M):.4f}"
  201:     supplied = float(written)
  202:     source = pnezd.parse_lines(
  203:         [f"1,{HOUGHTON_LATITUDE},{HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE},{written},GNSS"]
  204:     )
  205: 
  206:     point = run(
  207:         _vertical(
  208:             input_height_kind=HeightKind.ELLIPSOID, input_unit=unit, output_unit=unit
  209:         ),
  210:         source=source,
  211:     ).points[0]
  212: 
  213:     assert unit.to_meters(point.output_elevation) == pytest.approx(
  214:         HOUGHTON_H_M, abs=ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M
  215:     )
  216:     # The audit CSV's long-standing "Ellipsoid height (m)" cell reconstructs
  217:     # the user's own number â€” the one cell that would expose a double-counted
  218:     # separation immediately.
  219:     assert point.factors.ellipsoid_height == pytest.approx(
  220:         unit.to_meters(supplied), abs=1e-9
  221:     )
  222: 
  223: 
  224: # ==========================================================================
  225: # Horizontal mode: the Z is untouched, the factors are corrected.
  226: # ==========================================================================
  227: 
  228: 
  229: def test_horizontal_mode_writes_the_supplied_height_back_unchanged():
  230:     """The owner's instruction: horizontal mode never converts the Z.
  231: 
  232:     Compared as the WRITTEN STRING as well as the float, so this pins what a
  233:     CAD import would actually receive.
  234:     """
  235:     source = pnezd.parse_lines(_ZONE_ROW)
  236: 
  237:     orthometric = run(_horizontal(), source=source).points[0]
  238:     ellipsoid = run(
  239:         _horizontal(input_height_kind=HeightKind.ELLIPSOID), source=source
  240:     ).points[0]
  241: 
  242:     assert ellipsoid.output_elevation == orthometric.output_elevation
  243:     assert fmt.coordinate(ellipsoid.output_elevation, METERS) == fmt.coordinate(
  244:         orthometric.output_elevation, METERS
  245:     )
  246:     # Nothing was datum-tagged: horizontal mode asked no vertical question.
  247:     assert ellipsoid.vertical is None
  248:     # But the conversion DID happen, for the factors, and is on the record.
  249:     assert ellipsoid.ellipsoid_height is not None
  250: 
  251: 
  252: def test_horizontal_mode_stops_adding_the_separation_twice_to_the_factors():
  253:     """The ~5 ppm the owner asked to have fixed, measured.
  254: 
  255:     The elevation factor is R / (R + H + N). Given an ellipsoid height h the
  256:     correct denominator is R + h, because h IS H + N. Treating h as an
  257:     orthometric height computes R / (R + h + N) instead â€” a denominator short
  258:     by |N|, about 34 m in Michigan:
  259: 
  260:         delta = |N| / R ~ 34 / 6,372,000 ~ 5.3e-6
  261: 
  262:     The sign matters as much as the size: the wrong form makes the factor
  263:     LARGER, so grid distances come out long, systematically, on every line.
  264:     """
  265:     source = pnezd.parse_lines(_ZONE_ROW)
  266: 
  267:     wrong = run(_horizontal(), source=source).points[0]
  268:     right = run(
  269:         _horizontal(input_height_kind=HeightKind.ELLIPSOID), source=source
  270:     ).points[0]
  271: 
  272:     separation = right.factors.geoid_height
  273:     assert separation is not None and separation < 0.0
  274: 
  275:     # The corrected factor is computed from h itself: H + N == h.
  276:     assert right.factors.ellipsoid_height == pytest.approx(
  277:         HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M, abs=1e-9
  278:     )
  279:     assert right.factors.orthometric_height == pytest.approx(
  280:         HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M - separation, abs=1e-9
  281:     )
  282: 
  283:     predicted = -separation / MEAN_EARTH_RADIUS_M
  284:     measured = (
  285:         wrong.factors.elevation_factor - right.factors.elevation_factor
  286:     ) / right.factors.elevation_factor
  287:     assert measured == pytest.approx(predicted, rel=1e-3)
  288:     assert measured == pytest.approx(5.3e-6, abs=1.5e-6)
  289:     # The uncorrected factor is the LARGER one â€” distances long, not short.
  290:     assert wrong.factors.elevation_factor > right.factors.elevation_factor
  291: 
  292: 
  293: def test_the_default_leaves_every_existing_job_alone():
  294:     """ORTHOMETRIC is the default, and the default is the status quo.
  295: 
  296:     A job that never mentions the new setting must be identical to one that
  297:     states ORTHOMETRIC explicitly â€” the regression floor for every job this
  298:     program has ever run.
  299:     """
  300:     source = pnezd.parse_lines(_ZONE_ROW)
  301: 
  302:     silent = run(_horizontal(), source=source).points[0]
  303:     explicit = run(
  304:         _horizontal(input_height_kind=HeightKind.ORTHOMETRIC), source=source
  305:     ).points[0]
  306: 
  307:     assert silent.output_elevation == explicit.output_elevation
  308:     assert silent.factors == explicit.factors
  309:     assert silent.ellipsoid_height is None and explicit.ellipsoid_height is None
  310:

 succeeded in 572ms:
    1: """What the written outputs SAY about an ellipsoid-height conversion (WP-E3).
    2: 
    3: The rule this package exists to hold: a surveyor reading only the files, six
    4: months later, must be able to tell what kind of height went in and what came
    5: out. That is not decoration on a job whose Z column can differ by 34 m
    6: depending on the answer.
    7: 
    8: The two disclosures that carry the most weight, because the numbers alone
    9: cannot distinguish them:
   10: 
   11: * a HORIZONTAL job writes the ellipsoid height straight back into the Z
   12:   column, so the record must say so plainly - the export looks exactly like an
   13:   elevation export and is not one;
   14: * a point off the geoid tile in a vertical mode gets no Z at all, and the
   15:   ELEVATIONS section must not explain that with the VERTCON sentence, because
   16:   on an identity job no VERTCON grid was ever loaded. That sentence would be
   17:   the WP-R2-fix-C class of falsehood arriving through a third door, and the
   18:   design review caught it before this feature shipped.
   19: """
   20: 
   21: from __future__ import annotations
   22: 
   23: import csv
   24: 
   25: import pytest
   26: 
   27: from michspc.fileio import exports, geoid, pnezd
   28: from michspc.fileio.report import build_report
   29: from michspc.job import (
   30:     Direction,
   31:     JobSettings,
   32:     LongitudeConvention,
   33:     VerticalMode,
   34:     run,
   35: )
   36: from michspc.spc.units import INTERNATIONAL_FEET, METERS
   37: from michspc.spc.vertical import NAVD88, NGVD29, HeightKind
   38: from michspc.spc.zones import MI_NORTH
   39: from tests.fixtures.geoid_anchors import GEOID_ANCHORS
   40: 
   41: HOUGHTON_LATITUDE = 47.1211
   42: HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE = -88.5694
   43: N18_FIXTURE = next(
   44:     a.geoid_height_m
   45:     for a in GEOID_ANCHORS
   46:     if a.latitude == HOUGHTON_LATITUDE and a.longitude == HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE
   47: )
   48: HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M = 200.000 + N18_FIXTURE
   49: 
   50: 
   51: def _vertical(tmp_path=None, **overrides) -> JobSettings:
   52:     base = dict(
   53:         input_path=(tmp_path / "in.csv") if tmp_path else None,
   54:         output_directory=(tmp_path / "out") if tmp_path else None,
   55:         direction=Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY,
   56:         source_zone=None,
   57:         target_zone=None,
   58:         input_unit=METERS,
   59:         output_unit=METERS,
   60:         longitude_convention=LongitudeConvention.NEGATIVE_WEST,
   61:         vertical_mode=VerticalMode.VERTICAL,
   62:         source_vertical_datum=NAVD88,
   63:         target_vertical_datum=NAVD88,
   64:         geoid_model=geoid.GEOID18_MODEL,
   65:     )
   66:     base.update(overrides)
   67:     return JobSettings(**base)
   68: 
   69: 
   70: def _horizontal(tmp_path=None, **overrides) -> JobSettings:
   71:     base = dict(
   72:         input_path=(tmp_path / "in.csv") if tmp_path else None,
   73:         output_directory=(tmp_path / "out") if tmp_path else None,
   74:         direction=Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC,
   75:         source_zone=MI_NORTH,
   76:         target_zone=None,
   77:         input_unit=METERS,
   78:         output_unit=METERS,
   79:         longitude_convention=LongitudeConvention.NEGATIVE_WEST,
   80:         geoid_model=geoid.GEOID18_MODEL,
   81:     )
   82:     base.update(overrides)
   83:     return JobSettings(**base)
   84: 
   85: 
   86: def _at_houghton(height: float) -> pnezd.PnezdFile:
   87:     return pnezd.parse_lines(
   88:         [f"1,{HOUGHTON_LATITUDE},{HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE},{height:.3f},GNSS"]
   89:     )

  207:     for settings, source in (

  223:         assert cells["Input height kind"] == "ellipsoid"

  225: 
  226: def test_the_vertical_audit_row_keeps_its_arithmetic_closed():
  227:     """Source elevation + Vertical shift = Elevation, still, with the supplied
  228:     ellipsoid height in a column of its own.
  229: 
  230:     That is why "Source elevation" holds the DERIVED pre-shift height rather
  231:     than the raw h: this column has always meant "what the shift was applied
  232:     to", and the shift was applied to H.
  233:     """
  234:     result = run(
  235:         _vertical(
  236:             input_height_kind=HeightKind.ELLIPSOID, target_vertical_datum=NGVD29
  237:         ),
  238:         source=_at_houghton(HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M),
  239:     )
  240:     header = exports.audit_columns(result)
  241:     cells = dict(zip(header, exports.audit_rows(result)[1]))
  242: 
  243:     assert "Ellipsoid height in (m)" in header
  244:     assert float(cells["Ellipsoid height in (m)"]) == pytest.approx(
  245:         HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M, abs=0.0002
  246:     )
  247:     source_elevation = float(cells["Source elevation (m)"])
  248:     shift = float(cells["Vertical shift (m)"])
  249:     elevation = float(cells["Elevation"])
  250:     assert source_elevation + shift == pytest.approx(elevation, abs=0.0002)
  251:     # The supplied height is NOT what the shift was applied to.
  252:     assert source_elevation != pytest.approx(HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M, abs=1.0)
  253: 
  254: 
  255: def test_the_ellipsoid_height_column_reconstructs_the_supplied_value():

  258:         _vertical(input_height_kind=HeightKind.ELLIPSOID),
  259:         source=_at_houghton(HOUGHTON_ELLIPSOID_M),
  260:     )
  261:     cells = dict(zip(exports.audit_columns(result), exports.audit_rows(result)[1]))

"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command '$l=Get-Content '"'tests/test_ellipsoid_height.py'; for("'$i=1;$i -le 205;$i++){ if($i -le $l.Count){ '"'{0,5}: {1}' -f "'$i,$l[$i-1] } }' in C:\claude-projects\coord-convert
 succeeded in 573ms:
    1: """Ellipsoid-height input: the anchors, before the feature exists (WP-E1).
    2: 
    3: GNSS gives an ellipsoid height h; a plat needs an orthometric height H. The
    4: conversion is H = h - N, with N the geoid separation at the point.
    5: 
    6: **This module is written BEFORE the production code, deliberately** â€” the
    7: project's method holds that the work in a feature like this is the anchors,
    8: not the arithmetic. The arithmetic is one subtraction; what has to be true is
    9: that the separation it subtracts is NGS's, at the right position, with the
   10: right sign.
   11: 
   12: Two independent bodies of truth, and they check different things:
   13: 
   14: 1. **Fourteen NGS published Michigan benchmarks**
   15:    (``tests/fixtures/ellipsoid_height_anchors.py``), each carrying an NAVD 88
   16:    orthometric height AND a GEOID18 geoid height on its own datasheet. Their
   17:    sum is NGS's own ellipsoid height at that mark, so feeding h must give
   18:    back H. These exercise the whole path at real published control spread
   19:    over the entire state.
   20: 
   21: 2. **The Houghton geoid anchors**, which the geoid-to-geoid feature already
   22:    pins in the forward direction (DESIGN.md #50). Ellipsoid input is the
   23:    EXACT INVERSE of that arithmetic, and pinning both directions off one
   24:    frozen figure is what stops a sign error from hiding: at Houghton
   25:    N18 = -33.796, so h = 166.204 must give H = 200.000, which is #50's own
   26:    number read the other way.
   27: 
   28: **The sign is the thing.** In the conterminous United States the ellipsoid
   29: lies above the geoid, so N is negative throughout Michigan (about -33 to -37
   30: m), h is the SMALLER number, and H = h - N makes a height LARGER by about 34
   31: m. A conversion that made a Michigan height smaller has the sign backwards,
   32: and it would be wrong by 68 m â€” which is why both directions are pinned and
   33: why the falsification for these tests is h + N.
   34: """
   35: 
   36: from __future__ import annotations
   37: 
   38: import pytest
   39: 
   40: from michspc.fileio import geoid
   41: from tests.fixtures.ellipsoid_height_anchors import (
   42:     BENCHMARK_ANCHORS,
   43:     BENCHMARK_TOLERANCE_M,
   44: )
   45: from tests.fixtures.geoid12b_anchors import GEOID12B_ANCHORS
   46: from tests.fixtures.geoid_anchors import GEOID_ANCHORS
   47: 
   48: # The Houghton anchor, resolved from the frozen fixtures rather than retyped,
   49: # exactly as tests/test_geoid_swap.py resolves it - so this file and #50's
   50: # cannot drift apart about the same NGS figure.
   51: HOUGHTON_LATITUDE = 47.1211
   52: HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE = -88.5694
   53: N18_FIXTURE = next(
   54:     a.geoid_height_m
   55:     for a in GEOID_ANCHORS
   56:     if a.latitude == HOUGHTON_LATITUDE and a.longitude == HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE
   57: )
   58: N12B_FIXTURE = next(
   59:     a.geoid_height_m
   60:     for a in GEOID12B_ANCHORS
   61:     if a.latitude == HOUGHTON_LATITUDE and a.longitude == HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE
   62: )
   63: 
   64: # Two NGS figures printed to 0.001 m carry +/-0.0005 m apiece, and the
   65: # reader's worst measured residual against NGS's own service is sub-millimetre
   66: # (DESIGN.md #37). 0.0015 m is the derived bound, the same one #50 uses.
   67: ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M = 0.0015
   68: 
   69: 
   70: # ==========================================================================
   71: # The published benchmarks: h = H + N, so h - N must return H.
   72: # ==========================================================================
   73: 
   74: 
   75: @pytest.mark.anchor
   76: @pytest.mark.parametrize("anchor", BENCHMARK_ANCHORS, ids=lambda a: a.pid)
   77: def test_our_separation_matches_the_published_one_at_every_mark(anchor):
   78:     """The reader against NGS's own published GEOID18 figure at real control.
   79: 
   80:     This is the load-bearing half: once the separation agrees, the h -> H
   81:     subtraction has nothing left to get wrong but its sign, which the next
   82:     test pins.
   83:     """
   84:     ours = geoid.geoid_height(
   85:         anchor.latitude, anchor.longitude, geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL)
   86:     )
   87: 
   88:     assert ours == pytest.approx(anchor.geoid_height_m, abs=BENCHMARK_TOLERANCE_M)
   89:     # Negative everywhere in Michigan - the ellipsoid is above the geoid.
   90:     assert ours < 0.0
   91: 
   92: 
   93: @pytest.mark.anchor
   94: @pytest.mark.parametrize("anchor", BENCHMARK_ANCHORS, ids=lambda a: a.pid)
   95: def test_h_minus_n_returns_the_published_orthometric_height(anchor):
   96:     """The whole conversion, at fourteen published marks across Michigan.
   97: 
   98:     The published orthometric height's own precision cancels out of this
   99:     comparison â€” see the fixture module's derivation â€” so the tolerance is
  100:     purely the separation disagreement.
  101:     """
  102:     ours = geoid.geoid_height(
  103:         anchor.latitude, anchor.longitude, geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL)
  104:     )
  105: 
  106:     recovered = anchor.ellipsoid_height_m - ours
  107: 
  108:     assert recovered == pytest.approx(
  109:         anchor.orthometric_height_m, abs=BENCHMARK_TOLERANCE_M
  110:     )
  111:     # And it went UP: h is the smaller number in Michigan. This is the
  112:     # assertion that fails loudly on h + N, where the other one could still
  113:     # pass at a mark whose separation happened to be small.
  114:     assert recovered > anchor.ellipsoid_height_m
  115: 
  116: 
  117: def test_the_published_marks_span_the_state_and_all_three_zones():
  118:     """An anchor set clustered in one county would prove much less.
  119: 
  120:     Guards the fixture itself: a future edit that dropped the Upper Peninsula
  121:     marks would leave the suite green while halving what it covers.
  122:     """
  123:     latitudes = [a.latitude for a in BENCHMARK_ANCHORS]
  124:     longitudes = [a.longitude for a in BENCHMARK_ANCHORS]
  125: 
  126:     assert len(BENCHMARK_ANCHORS) >= 14
  127:     assert min(latitudes) < 42.0 and max(latitudes) > 47.0
  128:     assert min(longitudes) < -88.0 and max(longitudes) > -83.0
  129:     # Every PID distinct - a duplicated mark would inflate the count without
  130:     # adding a position.
  131:     assert len({a.pid for a in BENCHMARK_ANCHORS}) == len(BENCHMARK_ANCHORS)
  132: 
  133: 
  134: # ==========================================================================
  135: # The Houghton inverse: #50's own figure, read the other way.
  136: # ==========================================================================
  137: 
  138: 
  139: def test_the_houghton_anchor_inverts_the_geoid_swap_pin_under_geoid18():
  140:     """h = 166.204 -> H = 200.000, hand-derived from the frozen figure.
  141: 
  142:     #50 pins 200.000 m under GEOID12B becoming 199.968 m under GEOID18. This
  143:     is the same anchor from the other side: NGS's printed N18 is -33.796, so
  144:     the ellipsoid height of a 200.000 m NAVD 88 point at Houghton is
  145: 
  146:         h = H + N = 200.000 + (-33.796) = 166.204
  147: 
  148:     and converting it back must land on 200.000 exactly.
  149:     """
  150:     assert N18_FIXTURE == -33.796  # the frozen figure this derivation used
  151:     h = 200.000 + N18_FIXTURE
  152:     assert h == pytest.approx(166.204, abs=1e-9)
  153: 
  154:     ours = geoid.geoid_height(
  155:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE, HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE, geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL)
  156:     )
  157:     recovered = h - ours
  158: 
  159:     assert recovered == pytest.approx(200.000, abs=ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M)
  160: 
  161: 
  162: def test_the_houghton_anchor_inverts_under_geoid12b_too():
  163:     """The same point under the other model: N12B = -33.828, h = 166.172.
  164: 
  165:     Both models pinned, because the feature lets the user choose which one
  166:     converts, and a model mix-up moves the height by the 32 mm between them.
  167:     """
  168:     assert N12B_FIXTURE == -33.828
  169:     h = 200.000 + N12B_FIXTURE
  170:     assert h == pytest.approx(166.172, abs=1e-9)
  171: 
  172:     ours = geoid.geoid_height(
  173:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE,
  174:         HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE,
  175:         geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL),
  176:     )
  177:     recovered = h - ours
  178: 
  179:     assert recovered == pytest.approx(200.000, abs=ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M)
  180: 
  181: 
  182: def test_the_two_models_disagree_by_the_amount_50_recorded():
  183:     """32 mm at Houghton â€” so choosing the wrong model is a real error, and
  184:     the two anchors above are genuinely distinguishing rather than duplicates.
  185:     """
  186:     h = 166.204
  187:     under_18 = h - geoid.geoid_height(
  188:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE, HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE, geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID18_MODEL)
  189:     )
  190:     under_12b = h - geoid.geoid_height(
  191:         HOUGHTON_LATITUDE,
  192:         HOUGHTON_LONGITUDE,
  193:         geoid.default_grid(geoid.GEOID12B_MODEL),
  194:     )
  195: 
  196:     assert under_18 - under_12b == pytest.approx(
  197:         N12B_FIXTURE - N18_FIXTURE, abs=ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M
  198:     )
  199:     assert abs(under_18 - under_12b) == pytest.approx(0.032, abs=ANCHOR_TOLERANCE_M)

    1: """The results table's model.

    4: ``michspc.fileio.formatting`` â€” the same functions the written report and the

    7: 
    8: The model therefore performs no arithmetic at all. It does not round, it does
    9: not scale a unit, it does not decide what an absent value looks like. Those are
   10: domain decisions and they live one layer down.
   11: """
   12: 
   13: from __future__ import annotations
   14: 
   15: from dataclasses import dataclass
   16: 
   17: from PySide6.QtCore import QAbstractTableModel, QModelIndex, Qt
   18: from PySide6.QtGui import QBrush, QColor
   19: 
   20: from michspc.fileio import formatting as fmt
   21: from michspc.fileio import pnezd
   22: from michspc.fileio.exports import (
   23:     vertical_shift_and_sigma_m,
   24:     vertical_shift_heading,
   25:     vertical_sigma_heading,
   26: )
   27: from michspc.gui.controls import zone_label
   28: from michspc.job import (
   29:     Direction,
   30:     JobResult,
   31:     LongitudeConvention,
   32:     factors_geoid_model,
   33:     geoid_swap_models,
   34: )
   35: from michspc.spc.vertical import HeightKind, require_vertical_pair
   36: 
   37: COLUMNS: tuple[str, ...] = (
   38:     "Point",
   39:     "Northing",
   40:     "Easting",
   41:     "Elevation",
   42:     "Grid scale factor",
   43:     "Combined factor",
   44:     "Warnings",
   45: )
   46: 
   47: GEODETIC_COLUMNS: tuple[str, ...] = (
   48:     "Point",
   49:     "Latitude",
   50:     "Longitude",
   51:     "Elevation",
   52:     "Grid scale factor",
   53:     "Combined factor",
   54:     "Warnings",
   55: )
   56: """The same seven columns, named for what a State-Plane-to-geodetic job puts
   57: in them.
   58: 
   59: ``row_strings`` has always rendered columns 1 and 2 as a latitude and a
   60: longitude on that branch while the header still read "Northing" and "Easting",
   61: so the table labelled 42.73250000 as a northing (WP-R2 fix H). The columns
   62: themselves, their order, their alignment and their meaning are unchanged - only
   63: the two names move, and only for the one direction whose values are degrees.
   64: """
   65: 
   66: 
   67: # The table's shift and sigma column headings on a vertical job are the audit
   68: # CSV's own - ``exports.vertical_shift_heading`` and ``vertical_sigma_heading``
   69: # imported above, per the #17 standing choice of one wording on every surface.
   70: # They are functions of the unit, not constants: since the owner's units
   71: # instruction (2026-08-09) both headings carry the JOB'S INPUT UNIT, so the
   72: # table cannot restate the CSV's wording and drift from it.
   73: 
   74: 
   75: def _geodetic_display_columns(settings) -> bool:
   76:     """Whether the table's coordinate columns hold degrees for this job.
   77: 
   78:     True for a State-Plane-to-geodetic job, and for a vertical-only job whose
   79:     input is geodetic: that job's cells reproduce the input positions, so a
   80:     geodetic input keeps geodetic headings. The same branch
   81:     ``exports._geodetic_coordinate_columns`` takes for the files, restated
   82:     here only because this module may not import the file layer's private
   83:     names; ``row_strings`` renders through the identical formatters either
   84:     way, so the two cannot show different digits.
   85:     """
   86:     return settings.direction is Direction.ZONE_TO_GEODETIC or (
   87:         settings.direction is Direction.VERTICAL_ONLY
   88:         and settings.source_zone is None
   89:     )
   90: 
   91: 
   92: def _elevation_heading(settings, geoid_model_name: str | None) -> str:
   93:     """``Elevation (NAVD88, m)`` - the ordinary heading with the TARGET datum
   94:     and the OUTPUT unit.
   95: 
   96:     Vertical jobs only. The table's elevation cells hold the SHIFTED height
   97:     (``row_strings`` renders ``point.output_elevation``, which _convert_row
   98:     moved into the target datum), so a bare "Elevation" over them names the
   99:     number without saying which surface it is on - the WP-V7 review gate's
  100:     finding 4, assigned to WP-V8. The unit is the OUTPUT unit - the unit the
  101:     cells beneath are actually rendered in (``row_strings`` formats them with
  102:     ``settings.output_unit``), NOT the input unit that governs the shift and
  103:     sigma columns beside it. The datum and unit come from the settings the
  104:     job actually ran with, never from a dropdown's current state.
  105: 
  106:     Delegates to ``_datum_elevation_label`` rather than repeating its
  107:     template. The two were separate f-strings producing the same text until
  108:     the geoid tag arrived (DESIGN.md #52), which is one fact in two places -
  109:     exactly the arrangement that lets a panel and a table drift apart, and
  110:     the property amendment #26 spent a whole gate establishing is that they
  111:     cannot.
  112:     """
  113:     return _datum_elevation_label(
  114:         settings.target_vertical_datum, settings.output_unit, geoid_model_name
  115:     )
  116: 
  117: 
  118: def table_geoid_model_name(result: JobResult | None) -> str | None:
  119:     """The geoid model the table's elevation cells are ON, or None.
  120: 
  121:     Named only for a geoid-to-geoid job, where the output elevation's identity
  122:     is the model and nothing else: both ends carry the same datum and the same
  123:     unit, so without it the heading describes the input column equally well
  124:     (the owner's instruction, 2026-08-10). Every other job leaves it None -
  125:     a leveled NAVD 88 height does not belong to a geoid model (DESIGN.md #50),
  126:     and a horizontal job asked no vertical question at all.
  127: 
  128:     The transformation is resolved through the same registry lookup ``job.run``
  129:     and the record perform, and the swap decision through ``job.geoid_swap_models``,
  130:     so the heading cannot claim a swap the computation did not make.
  131:     """
  132:     if result is None:
  133:         return None
  134:     settings = result.settings
  135:     # GENERALISED from #52 (DESIGN.md #54): the model is named wherever the
  136:     # displayed orthometric height DEPENDS on a model - where a geoid change
  137:     # ran, or where the height was derived from an ellipsoid height. It is
  138:     # still not named beside a leveled height, which depends on no model
  139:     # (#50's recorded geodetic fact), so #52's two negative pins stand.
  140:     if settings.input_height_kind is HeightKind.ELLIPSOID:
  141:         model = factors_geoid_model(
  142:             settings,
  143:             require_vertical_pair(
  144:                 settings.source_vertical_datum, settings.target_vertical_datum
  145:             )
  146:             if settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations
  147:             else None,
  148:         )
  149:         return None if model is None else model.name
  150:     if not settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations:
  151:         return None
  152:     swap = geoid_swap_models(
  153:         settings,
  154:         require_vertical_pair(
  155:             settings.source_vertical_datum, settings.target_vertical_datum
  156:         ),
  157:     )
  158:     return None if swap is None else swap[1].name
  159: 
  160: 
  161: def columns_for(result: JobResult | None) -> tuple[str, ...]:
  162:     """The header row for a job's direction. ``COLUMNS`` until a job says else.
  163: 
  164:     An empty table shows the ordinary headings: nothing has been converted, so
  165:     naming the columns after a direction the user has not run would be the
  166:     interface answering a question it was not asked.
  167: 
  168:     A HORIZONTAL_AND_VERTICAL job renames the Elevation heading with the
  169:     target datum and gains the shift and sigma columns directly after it -
  170:     mirroring ``exports.audit_columns``, whose vertical block also sits
  171:     directly after Elevation, so the table and the audit CSV read in the same
  172:     order. A horizontal job's header is ``COLUMNS`` (or ``GEODETIC_COLUMNS``)
  173:     unchanged, to the string: horizontal mode asked no vertical question and
  174:     its table must not claim a datum nobody stated (plan section 1).
  175:     """
  176:     if result is None:
  177:         return COLUMNS
  178:     settings = result.settings
  179:     columns = list(
  180:         GEODETIC_COLUMNS if _geodetic_display_columns(settings) else COLUMNS
  181:     )
  182:     if settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations:
  183:         at = columns.index("Elevation")
  184:         columns[at] = _elevation_heading(settings, table_geoid_model_name(result))
  185:         # The shift and sigma headings carry the INPUT unit - the unit the
  186:         # elevations were supplied in, the owner's instruction (2026-08-09).
  187:         # In vertical-only mode input and output units are equal by
  188:         # construction; in Horizontal + Vertical they can differ, and the
  189:         # input unit still governs these two columns while the Elevation
  190:         # heading beside them names the output unit its own cells are in.
  191:         # ``row_strings`` converts the cells with the same unit object.
  192:         columns[at + 1 : at + 1] = [
  193:             vertical_shift_heading(settings.input_unit),
  194:             vertical_sigma_heading(settings.input_unit),
  195:         ]
  196:     return tuple(columns)
  197: 
  198: POINT_COLUMN = 0
  199: NORTHING_COLUMN = 1
  200: EASTING_COLUMN = 2
  201: ELEVATION_COLUMN = 3
  202: GRID_FACTOR_COLUMN = 4
  203: COMBINED_FACTOR_COLUMN = 5
  204: WARNINGS_COLUMN = 6
  205: """The HORIZONTAL layout's column indexes - the table every release since
  206: 0.1.0 has shown, unchanged by WP-V8. A vertical job's table carries two more
  207: columns after Elevation, so the model derives that job's warnings and
  208: alignment positions from its own header in ``set_result`` rather than from
  209: these constants; a fixed index applied to the wider table would paint the
  210: wrong cell amber."""
  211: 
  212: 
  213: def _warnings_index(columns: tuple[str, ...]) -> int:
  214:     """Where the Warnings column sits in this header."""
  215:     return columns.index("Warnings")
  216: 
  217: 
  218: def _right_aligned(columns: tuple[str, ...]) -> frozenset[int]:
  219:     """Which columns hold numbers, derived from the header itself.
  220: 
  221:     Every column except Point and Warnings - exactly the set the old
  222:     module-level constant froze for the seven-column layout (indexes 1-5),
  223:     restated as a rule so the vertical layout's shift and sigma columns
  224:     right-align without a second hand-kept index list to drift.
  225:     """
  226:     return frozenset(range(len(columns))) - {POINT_COLUMN, _warnings_index(columns)}
  227: 
  228: AMBER = QColor(255, 233, 178)
  229: """The one colour this table paints, and it means exactly one thing.
  230: 
  231: Amber = "look at this" (docs/method/METHOD.md section 5). It marks a cell that
  232: carries a warning. Red is reserved for a refusal â€” something that is actually
  233: wrong â€” and a refusal never produces a table row, because the job did not
  234: finish. Nothing else in this program is coloured.
  235: 
  236: Chosen light enough that the system's ordinary black text stays readable on it,
  237: because the palette is otherwise the native one.
  238: """
  239: 
  240: 
  241: def row_strings(result: JobResult) -> tuple[tuple[str, ...], ...]:
  242:     """Render one job's points as display strings.
  243: 
  244:     The northing/easting/elevation branch below is deliberately identical to
  245:     ``michspc.fileio.exports.clean_pnezd_rows``: when a job converts to geodetic
  246:     the first two columns hold a latitude and a longitude, not a grid
  247:     coordinate. The elevation is in the OUTPUT unit in every direction,
  248:     including that one - it is the only linear column left on a geodetic
  249:     export and the job record describes it in the output unit (WP-R2 fix A).
  250:     If those two ever diverge, the screen would be describing a different file
  251:     from the one written.
  252:     """
  253:     settings = result.settings
  254:     to_geodetic = _geodetic_display_columns(settings)
  255:     vertical = settings.vertical_mode.converts_elevations
  256: 
  257:     rows: list[tuple[str, ...]] = []
  258: 
  259:     for point in result.points:
  260:         if to_geodetic:
  261:             northing = fmt.latitude(point.output_northing)
  262:             easting = fmt.longitude(point.output_easting)
  263:         else:
  264:             northing = fmt.coordinate(point.output_northing, settings.output_unit)
  265:             easting = fmt.coordinate(point.output_easting, settings.output_unit)
  266: 
  267:         cells = [
  268:             point.point_id,
  269:             northing,
  270:             easting,
  271:             fmt.coordinate(point.output_elevation, settings.output_unit),
  272:             fmt.factor(point.factors.grid_scale_factor),
  273:             # combined_factor is None for a point with no usable elevation;
  274:             # fmt.factor renders that as "N/A", never as 1.0.
  275:             fmt.factor(point.factors.combined_factor),
  276:             "; ".join(warning.code.value for warning in point.warnings),
  277:         ]
  278: 
  279:         if vertical:
  280:             # Directly after the Elevation cell, under the two headings
  281:             # columns_for inserts at the same position. WHICH numbers these
  282:             # two cells carry is exports.vertical_shift_and_sigma_m's one
  283:             # statement, shared with the audit CSV, so this cell and the
  284:             # CSV's cannot disagree (#26's property): the datum reading's
  285:             # shift and sigma ordinarily, the SWAP shift with no sigma on a
  286:             # geoid-to-geoid point, neither where no reading exists (no
  287:             # elevation, or a coverage refusal - the warnings cell says
  288:             # which). A None renders N/A - never the raw figure, which is
  289:             # not an uncertainty (DESIGN.md #36). Both are converted into
  290:             # the INPUT unit - the same unit object ``columns_for`` built
  291:             # the two headings from (and the same one ``exports.audit_rows``
  292:             # converts its cells with), so heading and value cannot claim
  293:             # different units.
  294:             shift_m, sigma_m = vertical_shift_and_sigma_m(point)
  295:             cells[ELEVATION_COLUMN + 1 : ELEVATION_COLUMN + 1] = [
  296:                 fmt.vertical_quantity(shift_m, settings.input_unit),
  297:                 fmt.vertical_quantity(sigma_m, settings.input_unit),
  298:             ]
  299: 
  300:         rows.append(tuple(cells))
  301: 
  302:     return tuple(rows)
  303: 
  304: 
  305: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  306: # The single-point results display (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #26).
  307: #
  308: # Pure functions of a JobResult, and widgets are built from what they return.
  309: # Nothing below touches Qt, which is what lets the layout the owner approved be
  310: # asserted label by label without a QApplication - and what keeps the layout a
  311: # statement about the conversion rather than a property of some widget tree.
  312: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------
  313: 
  314: 
  315: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  316: class ResultValue:
  317:     """One labelled line of a results section. Both fields are display text."""
  318: 
  319:     label: str
  320:     text: str
  321: 
  322: 
  323: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  324: class ResultSection:
  325:     """One titled block of a single-point result: INPUT or OUTPUT."""
  326: 
  327:     title: str
  328:     values: tuple[ResultValue, ...]
  329: 
  330: 
  331: INPUT_TITLE = "INPUT"
  332: OUTPUT_TITLE = "OUTPUT"
  333: 
  334: WARNINGS_LABEL = "Warnings"
  335: 
  336: NO_WARNINGS = "none"
  337: """What the warnings line says when the conversion raised none.
  338: 
  339: Not a blank, which in a list of labelled values reads as an oversight, and not
  340: ``formatting.NOT_AVAILABLE``: "N/A" is reserved for a quantity that is genuinely
  341: absent and unknowable, and "this point raised no warnings" is neither - it is a
  342: result, and it is the good one.
  343: """
  344: 
  345: # The labels below are section-relative on purpose. exports.AUDIT_COLUMNS spells
  346: # the same quantities "Source northing", "Target northing", "Source grid scale
  347: # factor" and so on because the audit CSV is one flat row per point and has to
  348: # say which end it means. Here the INPUT and OUTPUT titles say it, so the
  349: # prefixes would be repeating the heading. Every other word is the audit CSV's
  350: # own, including the "(m)" on the two heights, so that the screen and
  351: # <stem>_full.csv name the same thing the same way.
  352: #
  353: # The one qualifier deliberately NOT carried over is the audit CSV's
  354: # "Longitude (neg west)". The DMS line beside it ends in a hemisphere letter,
  355: # and that letter is what states the convention (docs/DESIGN.md amendment #26):
  356: # -84 deg 33' 19.80000" W says both where the point is and how the number was
  357: # written, which is what the parenthesis existed to say.
  358: ZONE_LABEL = "Zone"
  359: UNITS_LABEL = "Units"
  360: NORTHING_LABEL = "Northing"
  361: EASTING_LABEL = "Easting"
  362: ELEVATION_LABEL = "Elevation"
  363: LATITUDE_LABEL = "Latitude"
  364: LATITUDE_DMS_LABEL = "Latitude (DMS)"
  365: LONGITUDE_LABEL = "Longitude"
  366: LONGITUDE_DMS_LABEL = "Longitude (DMS)"
  367: GRID_FACTOR_LABEL = "Grid scale factor"
  368: CONVERGENCE_LABEL = "Convergence"
  369: GEOID_HEIGHT_LABEL = "Geoid height (m)"
  370: ELLIPSOID_HEIGHT_LABEL = "Ellipsoid height (m)"
  371: ELEVATION_FACTOR_LABEL = "Elevation factor"
  372: COMBINED_FACTOR_LABEL = "Combined factor"
  373: 
  374: # The sigma row's label is ``exports.vertical_sigma_heading(settings.input_unit)``
  375: # - the audit CSV's own column heading, per the #17 standing choice of one
  376: # wording on every surface, now a function of the job's input unit rather than
  377: # a constant (owner's units instruction, 2026-08-09). It is a labelled row with
  378: # its own copy button, exactly like every other value, because a sigma is a
  379: # NUMBER a surveyor carries into a report - unlike the warnings, which are
  380: # prose and live in their own field (#30). That is also why it belongs in
  381: # ``single_point_clipboard_text`` (plan section 6's pin) while warnings do not.
  382: 
  383: VERTICAL_METHOD_LABEL = "Vertical method"
  384: """The label of a caveat row that no longer exists.
  385: 
  386: The row stood between the WP-V7 gate (its HIGH 1: the tab writes nothing, so
  387: a caveat not on screen does not exist for that user) and the owner's
  388: instruction of 2026-08-09 removing it (DESIGN.md #45) â€” the same ruling as
  389: #33/#34: verification is the user's responsibility, and the record carries
  390: the caveat for every written job. The constant remains so the suite can pin
  391: the ABSENCE by name â€” a row that quietly returned would be a decision nobody
  392: made."""
  393: 
  394: 
  395: def vertical_shift_label(transformation, unit) -> str:
  396:     """``Vertical shift NGVD29 -> NAVD88 (ift)`` - both datums in the label,
  397:     and the unit the value is rendered in.
  398: 
  399:     The datums are IN the label, not implied by the section, because the shift
  400:     is the one value on this panel that belongs to neither section alone: it is
  401:     what moved the INPUT elevation to the OUTPUT one, and a bare "Shift" copied
  402:     into a spreadsheet says nothing about which way. ``unit`` is the job's
  403:     input unit (the owner's instruction, 2026-08-09), the same one the value
  404:     beside this label is converted with.
  405:     """
  406:     return (
  407:         f"Vertical shift {transformation.source.code} -> "
  408:         f"{transformation.target.code} ({unit.code})"
  409:     )
  410: 
  411: 
  412: def geoid_swap_label(swap, unit) -> str:
  413:     """``Geoid change GEOID12B -> GEOID18 (ift)`` - a geoid-to-geoid point's
  414:     shift row label: the MODELS, not the datums.
  415: 
  416:     On a swap job the datum did not move - the transformation is an identity
  417:     - so a label naming the datums would describe the one thing that did not
  418:     happen. What moved the elevation is the change of geoid model, and the
  419:     label says so in the shape ``vertical_shift_label`` established: what
  420:     changed, which way, and the unit the value beside it is rendered in
  421:     (the job's input unit, the owner's 2026-08-09 instruction). The audit
  422:     CSV's generic ``Vertical shift (unit)`` / ``Shift sigma (unit)``
  423:     headings stay - they are true of both kinds of shift - so #17's
  424:     one-wording rule is not in play where the surfaces differ on purpose.
  425:     """
  426:     return (
  427:         f"Geoid change {swap.source_model_name} -> "
  428:         f"{swap.target_model_name} ({unit.code})"
  429:     )
  430:

    1: """Capture NGS published benchmarks carrying BOTH an NAVD 88 orthometric
    2: height and a GEOID18 geoid height, spread across Michigan's three zones."""
    3: import json, urllib.request, time
    4: 
    5: SEEDS = [
    6:     ("Monroe / Lake Erie",      41.92, -83.40),
    7:     ("Detroit",                 42.33, -83.05),
    8:     ("Lansing",                 42.73, -84.56),
    9:     ("Grand Rapids",            42.96, -85.67),
   10:     ("Port Huron",              42.97, -82.43),
   11:     ("Muskegon",                43.23, -86.24),
   12:     ("Saginaw",                 43.42, -83.95),
   13:     ("Ludington",               43.95, -86.45),
   14:     ("Traverse City",           44.76, -85.62),
   15:     ("Alpena",                  45.06, -83.43),
   16:     ("Mackinaw City",           45.78, -84.73),
   17:     ("Sault Ste. Marie",        46.50, -84.35),
   18:     ("Marquette",               46.55, -87.40),
   19:     ("Houghton",                47.12, -88.57),
   20: ]
   21: URL = ("https://geodesy.noaa.gov/api/nde/radial"
   22:        "?lat={lat}&lon={lon}&radius=6&type=BM")
   23: 
   24: out = []
   25: for name, lat, lon in SEEDS:
   26:     try:
   27:         with urllib.request.urlopen(URL.format(lat=lat, lon=lon), timeout=45) as fh:
   28:             data = json.load(fh)
   29:     except Exception as exc:
   30:         print(f"{name}: FAILED {exc}")
   31:         continue
   32:     good = [
   33:         r for r in data
   34:         if r.get("orthoHt") and r.get("geoidHt")
   35:         and (r.get("vertDatum") or "").strip() == "NAVD 88"
   36:         and (r.get("geoidModel") or "").strip() == "GEOID18"
   37:     ]
   38:     # the mark with the most decimal places on its orthometric height
   39:     good.sort(key=lambda r: (-len(r["orthoHt"].split(".")[-1]), r["pid"]))
   40:     if good:
   41:         r = good[0]
   42:         r["_seed"] = name
   43:         out.append(r)
   44:         print(f"{name}: {r['pid']} H={r['orthoHt']} N={r['geoidHt']} "
   45:               f"lat={r['lat']} lon={r['lon']}")
   46:     else:
   47:         print(f"{name}: none usable of {len(data)}")
   48:     time.sleep(0.4)
   49: 
   50: with open("bm_capture.json", "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
   51:     json.dump(out, fh, indent=2)
   52: print(f"\ncaptured {len(out)}")
